## 64. 25 Nov: request matching, hybrid (keyword -> fuzzy -> LLM) and fuzzy keyword scored from the cached LLM answers

- **What we tried:** two more request-matching methods on the same dev/test queries: `fuzzy_keyword` (the whole-word keyword rules, then a difflib fuzzy match on the aliases) and `hybrid` (keyword, then fuzzy, then the LLM only when neither finds a place).
- **Why:** the user wants an app that answers most requests offline and fast and calls the LLM only when it must; entry 59 showed keyword fast but weak on typos and indirect requests, the LLM accurate but about 5 s per request and online.
- **How:** `phase2/request-matching/match.py`: `fuzzy_match` compares every 1- to 3-word piece of the request with every alias of at least 4 characters (`difflib.SequenceMatcher` ratio >= the cut-off); `fuzzy_keyword_match` and `hybrid_match` return the stage that answered. `evaluate.py`: new `--phase tune-fuzzy` (grid 0.60 to 0.99 in 0.01 steps, dev rows only, no LLM; keeps the other `tuned.json` keys), per-method `stages`, `llm_share`, `offline_coverage`, `local_ms_keyword_or_fuzzy` and `ms_when_llm_called` in `results.json`, five methods in `results.png`. The hybrid's LLM step reads `llm_cache_v1.json` only; with `--no-llm-calls` a request whose answer is not cached is counted as missing and listed. Built and checked first in a scratch copy, then ported to main after entry 63. Ran `evaluate.py --phase tune-fuzzy`, then `evaluate.py --phase final --no-llm-calls` (`HF_HUB_OFFLINE=1`, the matching venv with sentence-transformers).
- **Result** (`results.json`, `tuned.json`):
  - Fuzzy cut-off 0.825 (dev 0.697 for `fuzzy_keyword`; best range 0.63 to 0.88), fixed before the final run.
  - Accuracy, easy 23 / hard dev 54 / hard test 51 / all test 62 / all 128: fuzzy_keyword 22/23, 34/54, 39/51, 49/62, 95/128; hybrid 22/23, 47/54, 46/51, 56/62, 115/128 (keyword 44/62 and llm 60/62 on test, unchanged). "None" on the hard set (precision, recall): fuzzy_keyword 22/42, 22/26; hybrid 22/22, 22/26. Supported hard requests wrongly rejected (of 79): fuzzy_keyword 20, hybrid 0.
  - Hybrid stages, all 128: keyword 75, fuzzy 11, LLM 42 (LLM share 0.3281, offline coverage 0.6719); test: 38, 5, 19 (share 0.3065). Latency: median 0.585 ms over all rows (mean 1813.466 ms, p95 6614.252 ms), 0.531 ms when answered locally, 5285.323 ms when the LLM is called (test 5938.766 ms; the cached wall clock of `claude -p`).
  - No new LLM call; no cached answer was missing.
  - The 13 hybrid misses are all right in the LLM alone: 11 answered wrongly by the keyword stage (e.g. `lecture theatre 3a` -> lt2a, `toilet on floor 6` -> male_toilet, `exit 10 on floor 4` -> exit_10) and 2 by the fuzzy stage (`lecure theater 2b` -> lt2a, `washrom femail` -> male_toilet).
  - Old methods: all 128 predictions of keyword, embed, llm, embed_doc, keyword_v1 and tokens identical to the committed `results.csv`; only the latency columns moved (keyword median 1.121 -> 0.474 ms, embed 9.297 -> 5.024 ms; the README quotes the new values).
- **Outcome:** success. The hybrid keeps two thirds of the requests offline at under 1 ms and reaches 56/62 on test against 60/62 for the LLM alone; its errors come from confident wrong local matches, not from the LLM.
- **What changed because of it:** `README.md` has both methods in the table and the hybrid section. No app work (by request).
- **Links:** `phase2/request-matching/match.py`, `evaluate.py`, `tuned.json`, `results.json`, `results.csv`, `failures.csv`, `confusion_pairs.csv`, `results.png`, `README.md`; the commit that adds this entry.

- **Outcome:** success after one failed run (KeyError) and one run with missing placeholders.
- **What changed because of it:** every figure and table in the pack includes the complete 25 Nov; `timeline.md` has the 25-Nov block for both folders.

## 66. 25 Nov: Tian's upload app retrained on 24 s1, 24 s2 and 25 Nov; leave-one-session-out evaluation over 3 sessions

- **What we tried:** make the session list of `phase2/new/upload/` data-driven so Dicta's complete 25-Nov pass trains the app, and extend `walksense.py evaluate` and `live.py check` from "24 s1 <-> 24 s2" to leave one session out over three sessions.
- **Why:** the app trained only on 24 Nov (`DATA`, `SESSIONS = ('sesi1','sesi2')`); iPhone19,2 now has two sessions (24 s1, 25 Nov), so it gets the first same-handset cross-session test (iPhone17,4 is still in 24 s2 only).
- **How:** minimal change, no UI work. `walksense.py`: `ALL_SESSIONS` = list of `{name, path}` (`sesi1`, `sesi2`, `25nov`), `session_dir()`, env var `WALKSENSE_SESSIONS=24nov` restores the old behaviour (outputs then get the tag, e.g. `eval_24nov.json`); `evaluate()` builds folds per held-out session and phone: `same_model` (train on other sessions with this phone model, Tian's rule) or `cross_handset` (same phone folder, another handset); raw x/y/z, 2 s windows, per floor, unchanged. Floor tracking now runs over each session's iPhone recordings. `route.py`, `live.py`, `test_*.py` use `session_dir()`; `live.py check` runs all ordered session pairs (standing time, walking dot) and adds replays of 25 Nov. `ws.DATA` kept for `phase2/new/smoothing/replay.py`. The old `out/eval.json` and `out/live_checks.json` were copied to `out/eval_2026-11-24.json` and `out/live_checks_2026-11-24.json` before they were overwritten. Check that the old behaviour is reproduced: `WALKSENSE_SESSIONS=24nov` on `evaluate` gave every old entry unchanged (compared key by key with the dated copy), and `live.py check` gave a byte-identical file (`cmp`). Map check: every 25-Nov stop node (26 per phone, 78 stops) is in `map/places.json` on its floor (script in the terminal, not saved); the places of all models equal the places of the map on both floors.
- **Result** (`out/eval.json`, xyz top-1 / top-3, chance about 0.077 with the true floor given): iPhone19,2 -> iPhone19,2 (same handset) 24 s1 -> 25 Nov 19/26 (21/26), 25 Nov -> 24 s1 18/27 (23/27); iPhone17,4 -> iPhone19,2 24 s2 -> 24 s1 17/27 (22/27) and 24 s2 -> 25 Nov 17/26 (21/26); iPhone19,2 (24 s1 + 25 Nov) -> iPhone17,4 24 s2 18/26 (25/26) (before: 17/26, 24/26); S24 unchanged at 11/26 and 10/27 (no S24 in 25 Nov); a55 24 s1 -> 25 Nov 5/26 and 25 Nov -> 24 s1 3/27; a56 14/26 and 13/27. Floor tracking (iPhone, time order): sesi1 27/27, sesi2 26/26, 25nov 26/26. `out/live_checks.json`: 25 Nov streamed live with the iPhone17,4 model found 26/26 stops, floor 26/26, top-1 17/26, top-3 21/26, lit 23 (16 right); same handset from 24 s1 only 19/26 (lit 25, 19 right), 24 s1 from 25 Nov only 18/27; walking dot 24 s2 -> 25 Nov right corridor 26/28, end within 3 m 27/28. Tests `test_walksense.py` and `test_live.py`: 6 tests, OK (130 s), one new test uploads a 25-Nov iPhone stop.
- **Outcome:** success. The 25-Nov numbers are on one more session, not a new method: n is 26 or 27 stops per fold and one stop is 3.7 to 3.8 points. a55 is the weakest phone (3/27 and 5/26).
- **What changed because of it:** the server, live mode and route graph train on all three sessions; iPhone19,2 now trains on 24 s1 + 25 Nov. `phase2/new/results-pack/` still quotes the old eval wording ("train on one 24-Nov session") and must be rebuilt (entry 65 rule) to pick up the new `up.*` keys.
- **Links:** `phase2/new/upload/walksense.py`, `live.py`, `route.py`, `server.py`, `test_walksense.py`, `test_live.py`, `README.md`, `out/eval.json`, `out/live_checks.json`, `out/eval_2026-11-24.json`, `out/live_checks_2026-11-24.json`; the commit that adds this entry.

## 68. 26 Nov: 36 verified byte-identical duplicate ZIPs deleted from our partial 25-Nov export

- **What we tried:** delete from `phase2/clean-data/2026-11-25-spots/raw/attachments/` only the files that are really byte-identical to Dicta's raw files (user-approved on 26 Nov; this was the open decision in entry 49 and the 25-Nov open question), and keep everything else.
- **Why:** the 36 round ZIPs were stored twice (ours and hers) and the CSV `duplicates_vs_2026-11-25.csv` only claimed the match. A match on timestamp, device or name is not enough, so the claim was re-checked from the bytes before anything was removed.
- **How:** a throwaway script (scratchpad, not in the repo; run with `python -I`) computed SHA-256 of all 52 files in our `raw/attachments/`, of all 116 top-level files in `../2026-11-25/raw/` and of every member of her ZIPs read in memory, nested containers included (`attachments.zip`, `canteen.zip`, `toilwt.zip`, `exit.zip`, `kntn.zip`, `exit (1).zip`; nothing extracted to disk). A file of ours counts as a duplicate only if its SHA-256 equals one of hers exactly. Checks on the result: the matching set equals the CSV's `byte_identical` rows, each row's sha256 equals the recomputed one, and each `dicta_clean_folder` exists and is non-empty in her `clean/` tree and her `manifest.csv` lists the file. For the 16 non-matches a second check looked for any same-device recording of hers starting within 30 s (the CSV used 2 s). Then `git rm` of the 36 files; `build_clean.py` rerun (the day-3 `run_all.sh` does this first anyway); `phone_map.py` and `data.py` rerun; CSV got the column `deleted_2026-11-26`; README and the `find_duplicates.py` docstring updated (do not rerun that script now, it would overwrite the CSV).
- **Result:** 872 distinct SHA-256 values over her files and members. 36 of 52 byte-identical (ip18 19, a56 17), every one a top-level file of hers (none only inside a container), every one reaching her clean tree. 0 files listed as `byte_identical` turned out not to be; 0 near-matches found among the other 16 (no same-device start within 30 s), so 16 kept: the 11 `DOC-20261125-WA0051` to `WA0061` S24 ZIPs, the WiGLE log `DOC-20261125-WA0062.gz`, and Tian's 4 spot ZIPs (`eska naik bawah`, `eska turun atas`, `eska turun bawah`, `sr9`). `raw/attachments/` went from 52 to 16 files. The rebuilt `clean/summary.json`: `recordings_found` 51 -> 15, `recordings_labelled` 43 -> 7 (4 high, 3 medium), floor check 11 -> 3 pairs (all agree). Day-3 loading after the deletion: `data.py` prints `25spots` ip16 4 stops (48 windows) and s24 3 stops (36 windows), 4 visits, unmapped none, the same as before; `phone_map.csv` unchanged (575 recordings, 0 problems); the `day3/cache/` files are identical before and after. `source_files.csv` still lists all 52 files (a record; rerunning `index_sources.py` would drop the 36 rows).
- **Outcome:** success. The deleted files are still in git history and byte-identical in `../2026-11-25/raw/`.
- **What changed because of it:** `raw/attachments/` holds only our own unique files; `clean/` (tracked tables, gitignored sensor folders) now holds only the 15 recordings of ours, the 36 round recordings live only in Dicta's tree. No day-3 table changed, so no rerun of `evaluate.py` was needed. Not done: `phase2/new/results-pack/` reads `2026-11-25-spots/clean/summary.json` (`recordings_found`, `recordings_labelled`, `per_track_device`) in `timeline_facts.json`, so its `timeline.md` would now say 15 recordings found, 7 labelled and, from the CSV, "36 of its files byte-identical ... and left out"; rerun `build.py` only after deciding the wording.
- **Links:** `phase2/clean-data/2026-11-25-spots/README.md`, `duplicates_vs_2026-11-25.csv`, `clean/summary.json`, `scripts/find_duplicates.py`; entries 42, 49; the commit that adds this entry.

- **What we tried:** (1) give the 11 S24 (SM-S9280) spot recordings `DOC-20261125-WA0051` to `WA0061` the names Michael gave them, replacing the 3 timing-based guesses of entry 42 (WA0052, WA0055, WA0056); (2) fold in three user confirmations from the same day (the 3 medium name mappings, the real names of the two disabled toilets, and a floor correction for WA0052); (3) rerun the whole day-3 pipeline (default and `--split20`), both sheets, the playground checks and the results pack, and compare with the committed numbers.

  - WA0060 (68.3 s, 4 time events = paused and resumed) loads as one stop: the day-3 loader read 13 five-second windows from it (the other S24 stops give 12); nothing was dropped, so it is kept.

Place ids `disabled_toilet` and `disabled_toilet_4F` are unchanged (renaming would break history and comparisons). `phase2/new/day3/config.json` has a new `place_display` block (two entries); `data.place_display()` / `data.relabel_sheet()` apply it to the playground pages (`playground.py` -> `P.place_labels`, `playground_template.html` uses `P.pn` for shown text only; the checked kNN block is untouched), the day-3 figures (`visualise.py`), the results-pack heat-map labels (`results-pack/build.py`), and both Excel sheets (`perf_sheet.py`, `gaps_sheet.py`). `phase2/new/routing/build_graph.py` and `graph.json` names, and `phase2/request-matching/build_destinations.py` / `destinations.csv` (names plus aliases "male disabled toilet 5f", "mixed disabled toilet", "mixed disabled toilet 4f", "unisex accessible toilet") now say "Male disabled toilet (5F)" and "Mixed disabled toilet (4F)". The LLM runs were not repeated (only these text fields changed); `phase2/new/upload/map/places.json` has only keys and coordinates (no label text), so only a comment in `make_map.py` was edited.
  - Rerun: `sh phase2/new/day3/run_all.sh` and `--split20` (exit 0 both). Before the rerun the committed `out/` and `out-split20/` were copied aside to compare; dated copies of the old playgrounds and sample predictions are kept as `*_2026-11-25d.*` in `out/` and `out-split20/`. `perf_sheet.py` appended the tabs `2026-11-26 default s24 names` and `2026-11-26 split20 s24 names` (new Progress rows); `hard_places.py --label after_s24_names out out-split20`; `gaps_sheet.py --perf-tab "2026-11-26 gaps s24 names"` rewrote `out/data_gaps.xlsx` (copy: `WalkSense data gaps 2026-11-26.xlsx` in Downloads, the 2026-11-25 copy kept). `playground_check.py` for both variants (default, and `DAY3_VARIANT=split20`): exit 0, no mismatch lines. `results-pack/build.py`: 1521 facts, 10 figures, 0 missing items; check "per-sample LOSO 122/295 equals fused_knn.json 122/295: True". Grouped by stop: 101/287 -> 106/295. split20 (20 s pieces): 385/818 = 47.1% -> **375/842 = 44.5%**; grouped by stop 301/818 -> 339/842. The S24 pressure falls by 11.4 hPa across the 24 minutes with steps of up to 4.3 hPa between neighbouring stops, so it neither supports nor contradicts a floor; the S24 5F values (1009.71 to 1019.31) and 4F values (1007.94 to 1012.48) overlap. ## 70. 26 Nov: the upload app's map becomes a vector floor plan in metres (picture kept as fallback)

- **What we tried:** replace the sketch picture on Tian's upload page (`phase2/new/upload`, entry 25) with a real floor plan: one metric frame per floor with corridors, room footprints, the 26 places, every corridor with its length and source, escalators/lift with their direction and floor link, scale bar and north arrow, light and dark mode, usable on a phone. Every existing behaviour had to stay (lit circle at >= 80%, dashed "maybe" ring, walking dot from `route.py`, current floor, uploads, live mode).
- **Why:** the user wanted "a proper floor plan with distances and labels instead of running from a pic".
- **How:** `phase2/new/upload/map/make_floorplan.py`. (1) `trace` (needs `pymupdf`) reads `phase2/clean-data/2026-11-24/floor-sketch-v1.pdf`: the red dots and black corridor lines as exact vector coordinates, the typed 4F metre marks, and the Google indoor-map screenshot under the drawing, which is split by Google's flat colours (rooms, corridors, toilets, escalator/stair blocks) and traced to polygons -> `map/sketch_trace.json`. (2) Build (numpy + scipy): a scale + rotation + shift from 5F screenshot pixels to the metres of `phase2/map/coordinates_meters.csv` (x east, y north) fitted on the 8 places with Google pins; the 4F screenshot joined to the 5F pixels through the two Google markers visible on both (City Express, Y5ZONE); then least squares over all places and corridor bends with the pins (sigma 0.25 m), the `phase2/new/routing/graph.json` lengths (0.3 m measured, max(0.5 m, 15%) steps, 4 m guess) and the sketch position (1.5 m) -> `map/floorplan.json`. Display names: 5F "Male disabled toilet (5F)", 4F "Mixed disabled toilet (4F)" (user-confirmed 26 Nov); `gate` = `entrance_exit`, `y5302_point2` = `y5302_back`. `map/floorplan.js` (no libraries) draws it as SVG; `index.html` uses it by default with a **Picture** toggle back to the PNG, zoom buttons and drag-to-pan; `server.py` serves the two new files only. The walking dot is not recomputed: `route.py` still gives a position in `places.json` pixels, the page projects it onto the pixel segment between the two places to get the fraction and places the dot at that fraction of the corridor drawn in metres. `route.py`, `places.json`, the PNGs and `make_map.py` are unchanged. Checked in headless Chromium (playwright-core with the cached Chromium; the Playwright MCP could not find Chrome) on `server.py --port 8010`.
- **Result** (`floorplan.json` > `fit`): 0.0887 m per screenshot pixel, rotation 1.1 deg (north-up, so the north arrow is real), sketch dots vs Google pins 0.75 m RMS (worst Exit 5, 1.15 m); 4F -> 5F pixels scale 0.997, rotation -0.08 deg. 31 same-floor corridors: 8 measured, 22 steps, 1 guess. Edge residual (drawn minus `graph.json`) RMS 5F 1.04 m, 4F 0.33 m; measured edges within 0.06 m; largest: Exit 10 - up escalator +3.57 m (the guess), Lift - Y5-401 -1.54 m, up - down escalator -1.10 m, Y5-302 back - lift -1.19 m, 4F Gate - Exit 5 -0.99 m (full table in `phase2/new/upload/README.md`, "The map v2"). Places moved more than 1 m from their sketch dot by the fit: 5F down escalator 2.63 m, Y5-401 2.42 m, up escalator 2.22 m, lift 1.8 m, Exit 5 1.15 m. Browser check: a real 25-Nov stop upload (`Y5202 2026-11-25 17-55-10.zip`) lit Y5-202 on 5F and `Lt1a 2026-11-25 19-07-11.zip` lit LT1A on 4F (both 100%, meaningless as accuracy because 25 Nov is in the training data); walking dots from `route.Dot` replayed on three 25-Nov walks (canteen -> Exit 5, study rooms -> Y5-202, 4F LT1B -> LT1A) landed on the drawn corridors; the lift/escalator badge switched floors; picture fallback still shows the PNG; 390 px phone width has no horizontal scroll; no console errors except the existing favicon 404. `python -m unittest test_walksense.py test_live.py`: 7 tests OK in 85.5 s (`test_walksense.py` 6, one of them new: every place and every `route.py` corridor is in `floorplan.json`, and the build files are not served; `test_live.py` 1).
- **Outcome:** success as a UI and as a single metric map; the geometry is only as good as its sources. 4F has no surveyed point. The sketch's metre marks and the step lengths disagree on the 5F escalator triangle (sketch 3.9 / 4.9 m, steps 10.3 / 8.1 m) and on Lift - Y5-401 - Y5-302 back (sketch 4 + 9 m, steps 10.3 + 5.9 m); the fit splits the difference. Room-to-place matches are our reading of the Google map.
- **What changed because of it:** the upload/live page shows distances, room blocks and the floor links on a scaled plan; the old picture is one tap away. The team can fix a point or a length by editing `floorplan.json` (README explains the fields) or rebuild from the sources.
- **Links:** `phase2/new/upload/map/make_floorplan.py`, `floorplan.json`, `floorplan.js`, `sketch_trace.json`; `phase2/new/upload/index.html`, `server.py`, `test_walksense.py`, `README.md` ("The map v2"); `phase2/new/upload/screenshots/v2-*.png`; entries 25, 52; the commit that adds this entry.
- **Landed:** branch `feat/vector-map` (916d231) merged into main with merge commit ad62427 on 26 Nov (renumbered from the provisional 90 to 70; the only conflict was this log). The plan's place names already match `day3/config.json` `place_display` ("Male disabled toilet (5F)", "Mixed disabled toilet (4F)"); no code change was needed.

## 71. 26 Nov: search in the upload app (Gemini if a key is set, offline keyword -> fuzzy otherwise) with the A* route on the floor plan

- **What we tried:** make search and the LLM "ready so that all I do is paste the Gemini API key" (user, 26 Nov). (1) One key file: repo-root `.env` (gitignored), `.env.example` committed with an empty `GEMINI_API_KEY=`. (2) A Gemini backend for request matching with the same grounded prompt as the Claude `llm` method, plus `hybrid_gemini` (keyword -> fuzzy -> Gemini) and a capped evaluation. (3) A search bar in Tian's upload app that answers room names and free questions ("where can I study"), says politely when a place is outside our area, and routes there from where you are. No real API call was made: no key exists yet.
- **Why:** the proposal's request matching (objective #9) and routing were separate scripts; the app had neither. The demo runs on a laptop hotspot, so search must work with no internet.
- **How:** `phase2/request-matching/gemini.py`: standard-library client (urllib, no SDK) for `POST https://generativelanguage.googleapis.com/v1beta/models/<model>:generateContent`, key only in the `x-goog-api-key` header, 8 s timeout (`GEMINI_TIMEOUT_S`), errors turned into plain English (no key / key rejected / quota / unknown model / no internet / timeout / empty reply); also the stdlib `.env` loader (real environment variables win). Model and endpoint checked on Google's docs on 26 Nov 2026 (aistudio.google.com/docs/models, /api-reference/generate-content, /docs/pricing): default `gemini-3.5-flash-lite` (stable, described as the fastest and most cost-effective 3.5 model, free tier listed); `gemini-2.5-flash` is deprecated. `match.py`: `gemini` and `hybrid_gemini` methods send `LLM_SYSTEM` + `llm_prompt` unchanged, cache every real reply in `llm_cache_gemini_v1.json` (committed empty), never cache errors; `ASK_SYSTEM` / `ask_prompt` / `parse_ask` = the app's variant (same place block, JSON `{place_id, answer}`, one sentence, only facts from the list, NONE + polite "not in the area WalkSense covers"). `evaluate.py --method gemini|hybrid_gemini`: all 128 rows, hard `--max-calls` (default 150), stops calling after the first error, writes only `out/gemini_results.json` and `out/gemini_failures_<method>.csv`; no key = a message and exit 0. App: `phase2/new/upload/search.py` (Gemini first; on any failure keyword -> fuzzy from `match.py`; Gemini skipped for 60 s after no internet / timeout / quota, until restart after a rejected key) and `GET /api/search?q=&from=` + `GET /api/search/places` in `server.py`; the key never reaches the browser. Route: `phase2/new/routing/route.py` imported by file path (as `astar_route`, because `upload/route.py` is the walking dot, unchanged). Start = the **From** list, else the lit place (live, else the last uploaded stop), else the 4F entrance gate. Graph ids vs map keys: explicit 26-row table `search.GRAPH_TO_MAP` (map keys repeat across floors, e.g. `exit5_door`, and the 4F lift is also `lift_stairs5`), checked by a test against `floorplan.json` `graph_id`, `destinations.csv` `map_key` and `places.json`. `index.html`: sticky search bar on the top edge, result card at the top of the page (no popup), orange route line along the `floorplan.js` corridors (`FloorPlan.corridor` now exported), start ring and end dot, steps list. `check_gemini.py`: one tiny call, prints OK + model or the plain-English error.
- **Result:** tests (all with a fake HTTP layer, no key): `phase2/request-matching/test_gemini.py` 13 OK; `phase2/new/upload/test_search.py` 10 OK (Gemini good reply, NONE, garbage, timeout, HTTP 400/403/429, fuzzy and none offline, cross-floor route, the id table, `/api/search` through a real `server.py` handler on a free port, key absent from every response); existing `test_walksense.py` + `test_live.py` 7 OK; `routing/test_route.py` 4 OK. `check_gemini.py` with the empty key prints "NOT SET" and how to fix it; `evaluate.py --method hybrid_gemini` prints the no-key message and exits 0. Server on port 8010 with no key (`phase2/new/upload/out/search_samples_nokey.json`): "y5401" -> keyword, `y5401`, 64.8 m / 57.8 s from the default start (4F gate, via the lift); "where can I study" -> keyword, `sr9`, 67.2 m / 71.7 s (via the 4F up escalator); "toilet on 4th floor" -> keyword, `disabled_toilet_4F`, 17.8 m / 13.7 s. Headless Chromium screenshots `phase2/new/upload/screenshots/v3-search-*.png` (no console errors; no horizontal scroll at 390 px after the From list was narrowed).
- **Outcome:** success for the offline path and the wiring; the Gemini path is tested only against a fake server. Not yet known: Gemini's real accuracy on the 128 queries, its latency over the hotspot, and whether the free tier is enough.
- **What changed because of it:** once a key is pasted into `.env` and `server.py` restarted, search answers with Gemini; without one it answers offline and the page says which method answered. Request matching can be scored with Gemini on the same prompt as Claude.
- **Links:** `.env.example`, `.gitignore`; `phase2/request-matching/gemini.py`, `match.py`, `evaluate.py`, `test_gemini.py`, `llm_cache_gemini_v1.json`, `README.md`; `phase2/new/upload/search.py`, `server.py`, `index.html`, `map/floorplan.js`, `check_gemini.py`, `test_search.py`, `README.md` ("Search with Gemini: setup"), `out/search_samples_nokey.json`, `screenshots/v3-search-*.png`; entries 52 to 59, 64, 70; branch `feat/gemini-search` (the commit that adds this entry).

## 72. 26 Nov: 25-Nov night run (Dicta, Tian, Chris) added as sessions `25night` and `25night_tian`; day-3 rerun, sheets, upload app retrained

- **What we tried:** add the second collection run of 25 Nov (about 21:40 to 00:40 on 26 Nov) as new data: Dicta's 59 iPhone19,2 (ip18) recordings from her WhatsApp export, Tian's 56 iPhone17,4 (ip16) files and Chris's 50 + 8 SM-A566B (a56) files plus his WiGLE log (`WigleWifi_20261126001048.csv.gz`), downloaded as loose files. Then rerun the day-3 pipeline (default and split20), the sheets, the playground, the results pack and Tian's upload app. Michael's two S24 takes at 20:2x and four of Tian's at 20:2x were before the run and went to `2026-11-25-spots`.
- **Why:** a third same-handset session for ip18 and a56, the first same-handset test for ip16 (iPhone17,4 was only in 24 s2), and second takes at the weak places (the extra takes 00:15 to 00:40 were aimed at sr9, y5202, exit 10, the 5F male disabled toilet, the lift/stairs on both floors, Y5302's back door and Y5401).
- **How:**
  - Every input is an untrusted download: the export was extracted in Python (`zipfile`, path check) into its own new folder in the scratchpad, every script ran with `python -I`, nothing from the inputs was executed, and no chat text was read or kept.
  - New reusable script `phase2/clean-data/2026-11-25-night/scripts/ingest_export.py <export.zip | folder> [--since] [--dry-run]`: SHA-256 of every file against every `.zip`/`.gz` in the repo (including members of container zips), byte-identical copies inside the source kept once, files outside the run window listed but not copied, the WhatsApp `0000xxxx-` prefix stripped (bytes unchanged), and one proposed row per recording in `labels_night.csv`. A row is `ok` when its START is within 150 s of an anchor take of the same type and its own file name names the same place, otherwise `review`.
  - Labels come from each collector's own file names (team rule; translations and every context-resolved name with its reason are in `labels_night.csv` and the night README). Dicta and Chris walked in step (track `round`, session `25night`). Tian walked the loop alone, faster and in his own order, so he is his own track (`tian`, session `25night_tian`), as for 24 Nov s1/s2.
  - `scripts/build_night.py` writes one clean tree per track in Dicta's layout (`round/clean`, `tian/clean`). `scripts/nightlib.py` holds the shared helpers. Times after midnight are written `24:mm:ss` so every reader keeps the order without code changes.
  - Two checks: `scripts/check_tian_lt.py` (magnetometer of Tian's doubtful 4F stops against his 24-Nov takes) and `scripts/check_field.py` (median |B| per phone and session, offset against the first 25-Nov run).
  - Day 3:
    - `config.json` has sessions `25night` and `25night_tian` with names `names_25night.csv` (Dicta's 25-Nov name map).
    - `run_all.sh` runs `build_night.py` first.
    - `visualise.py` has a colour and marker for the new session.
    - Fix in `phone_map.py`: the raw path is now absolute, because `raw/` is not under the track root and `relative_to` raised `ValueError` on the first run.
  - Spots (`2026-11-25-spots`):
    - `WA0075` = male toilet 5F (chat name 'toilet cowo 2026-11-25 20-23-09.zip').
    - `WA0076` = escalator down 5F (byte-identical to 'esc turun lvl 5 2026-11-25 20-24-55.zip'). Both are in `labels_override.csv`.
    - Tian's 4 files at 20:23 to 20:27 are labelled from their names. `build_clean.py` got a `y5302 back` rule (`Y5302B` -> `y5302_back`), so a name that says "back" no longer becomes `y5302`.
  - Before the rerun, dated copies: `day3/out*/knn_playground_2026-11-26a.html`, `fused_sample_predictions_2026-11-26a.csv`, `upload/out/eval_2026-11-26.json`, `live_checks_2026-11-26.json`.
  - Commands: `sh phase2/new/day3/run_all.sh` and `--split20`; `perf_sheet.py` (two new tabs); `gaps_sheet.py --perf-tab "2026-11-26 gaps night"`; `hard_places.py --label after_night out out-split20`; `playground_check.py` (both variants); `results-pack/build.py`; in `upload/`: `walksense.py evaluate`, `live.py check`, `python -m unittest test_walksense.py test_live.py`.
- **Result:**
  - **Data** (`build_summary.json`, `labels_night.csv`):
    - 169 recordings + 1 WiGLE log in `raw/`. Of the 178 loose Downloads files, the 51 iPhone19,2 files from Dicta's export, the a56 file 'y5202 2026-11-25 17-55-06.zip' (already in the repo) and 2 byte-duplicates were skipped.

Losses: exit_10_4F 6/6 -> 3/6, exit_10 6/6 -> 4/6, escalator_down 2/6 -> 0/6, disabled_toilet_4F 3/6 -> 1/6, -1 at lift_stairs_4F and lt1b.

After the 19-minute gap (23:17 -> 23:36) its reference restarted on 4F pressure while it still said 5F. The day-3 step tracker scores 29/32 on the same session.

The takes are kept, not dropped (no data deleted); whether to exclude or recalibrate them is a team decision.

## 73. 26 Nov: Tian's 26-Nov morning pass added as session `26tian` (labels from his chat captions); day-3 rerun, gaps sheet, upload app retrained

- **What we tried:** add Tian's morning pass of 26 Nov (iPhone17,4 = ip16, 09:30 to 10:39, both floors) as a new day-3 session, rerun day 3 (default and split20), the gaps sheet, the playgrounds and Tian's upload app, and use his new LT stops to check his five doubtful night takes.
- **Why:** a third same-handset session for ip16 (24 s2, `25night_tian`, now `26tian`), and this morning he recorded LT2A, LT2B, LT1B and LT1A on the same phone, which the night-run open question needed.
- **How:**
  - Inputs are untrusted downloads: 52 phyphox ZIPs (`exit 5 2026-11-26 09-31-47.zip` and 51 `point 2026-11-26 HH-MM-SS.zip`) and the chat export `GE2340 (1).zip`, extracted in Python into its own scratch folder; every script ran with `python -I`; nothing from the inputs was executed. The chat text is not in the repo.
  - `phase2/clean-data/2026-11-26-tian/scripts/ingest_tian26.py <folder> <chat.txt>`: SHA-256 of every ZIP against every `.zip`/`.gz` in the repo (the night run's `repo_hashes`; all 52 new), all 52 iPhone17,4; each file matched to Tian's first `<document omitted>` line at or after its save time, failing unless the match is one-to-one, in order, each gap 0 to 60 s and each caption before the next file. Captions -> places through one table in the script (reason per caption), kind checked against the duration. Writes `labels_tian26.csv` and copies the ZIPs to `raw/`.
  - `scripts/build_tian26.py` (the night build for one phone) writes `clean/` in Dicta's layout; `run_all.sh` runs it first. `config.json` has session `26tian` (same structure as `25night_tian`, names `names_25night.csv`); `visualise.py` has a colour and marker for it.

## 74. 26 Nov: floor rules when the barometer is missing or a long gap hides a floor change: current rules reproduced, fallbacks pre-registered, then scored on held-out sessions (offline only)

(Landed as entry 74; the pre-registration file and code comments still say "entry 75, provisional": they were left unchanged on purpose so the pre-registration commit a884c97 stays as written.)

- **What we tried:** find a floor rule that survives the 25-night failure (Dicta's iPhone logged no pressure 22:43 to 23:17, across the 5F -> 4F change; the upload app showed 5F at 12 of her 4F stops, 20/32) without breaking the sessions where pressure is complete, and see whether anything helps the phones that never log pressure (a55, a56). Four fallback ideas: (a) the magnetic kNN's own floor vote, (b) detecting the escalator/lift/stair ride from the motion sensors, (c) gap handling, (d) pressure when present, else the magnetic vote, with hysteresis.
- **Why:** open questions of entries 30 and 72 ("add a guard for long pauses and pre-register it before testing it"; "the upload app's floor tracker should handle a recording without pressure, and a long gap, at a floor change").
- **How:**
  - New folder `phase2/new/floor/` (analysis only). The upload app (`upload/walksense.py`) and day 3 (`day3/data.py`, `day3/evaluate.py`) are imported read-only; nothing in `upload/` or `day3/` was changed. `floorlib.py` builds one table of every recording (stops and walks) per session and phone slot with its edge pressures; 23 Nov comes from the day-3 visit table (stops only).
  - Step 1, `baseline.py` -> `out/baseline.json`: R0 (upload app, `Session.track_floor`: 0.40 hPa against the median of the last 6 edge readings, reference emptied after 15 min without pressure) and R1 (day-3 step tracking on stop medians, threshold from the other sessions) rerun from their own code.
  - Step 2, `PREREGISTRATION.md`, committed with step 1 in a884c97 **before any candidate was run**: the rules, the grids (a: k in {5, 15, 25}; c1: gap T in {5, 10, 15, 30, 60 min, never}; c2: drift-extrapolated reference, fixed; d: k x P in {0.5..0.9} x H in {1, 2} x M in {0, 3}), the selection criterion and tie-breaks, the split (tuning = 23nov, 24s1, 24s2, 25nov; held-out = 25night, 25night_tian, 26tian, 25spots), the masked tuning runs (pressure deleted for every recording within 20 min of the floor change, to imitate Dicta's dropout) and the decision rule.
  - Step 3, `candidates.py`: tunes on the tuning units only (`out/tuning.json`), freezes the parameters (`out/params.json`), scores every rule on every session x phone slot (`out/scores.json` with the wrong stops and the magnetic votes, `out/table.csv`), applies the decision rule (`out/decision.json`), runs the (b) data check (`out/motion_check.json`) and draws `fig_scores.png`, `fig_25night_timeline.png`, `fig_motion_check.png`. Results and recommendation: `phase2/new/floor/README.md`.
  - Data: the gitignored clean CSVs of the night, spots and 26-Nov trees were copied from the main tree, and the day-3 feature cache (`features_e3dbec0cff7b.pkl`) from the a56 worktree; nothing was rebuilt. Both scripts take a few seconds.
- **Result:**
  - **Reproduction** (`out/baseline.json`, `all_match` true): R0 27/27, 26/26, 26/26, 20/32, 23/23, 25/25 (24 s1, 24 s2, 25 Nov, 25night, 25night_tian, 26tian) = `upload/out/eval.json`; R1 = every `day3/out/results.json` `floor` number (24s1 23/27, 25night 29/32, 25spots 12/18, s24 13/27, 17/26, 9/18, the rest all right).
  - **(b) not testable** (`out/motion_check.json`): in 301 iPhone recordings with pressure the largest start-to-end change is 0.19 hPa; no floor change happened inside any recording (the rides were between recordings), so there is nothing labelled to build a motion detector on.
  - **Frozen parameters** (`out/params.json`, `out/tuning.json`): (a) k = 5 (211 of 280 tuning stops); c1 T = never (202 of 242, against 190 for 5 to 30 min and 196 for 60 min); (d) k = 15, P = 0.7, H = 2, M = 3 (359 of 401).
  - **Held-out stops on the right floor**, R0 / R1 / c1 / c2 / a / d (`out/scores.json`):
    - 25night ip18: 20 / 29 / 20 / 28 / 25 / **30** of 32 (walks 14 / 24 / 14 / 22 / 22 / 25 of 27).
    - 25night a56: 19 / 19 / 19 / 19 / 17 / 19 of 32.
    - 25night_tian ip16: 23 / 23 / 23 / 23 / 21 / 23 of 23. 26tian ip16: 25 / 25 / 25 / 25 / 24 / 25 of 25.
    - 25spots ip16: 4 / 4 / 4 / 4 / 5 / 5 of 6. 25spots s24: 7 / 8 / 7 / 7 / 9 / 10 of 13.
  - **Tuning units** (in-sample for the parameters): 24s1 ip18 27 / 23 / 23 / 23 / 19 / 27 of 27 (R1, c1, c2 lose the 4 weather stops after the break); masked 24s1 15 / 21 / 21 / 21 / 19 / 22, masked 25nov 14 / 20 / 20 / 20 / 24 / 24 of 26, masked 24s2 14 / 14 / 14 / 14 / 17 / 14 of 26; a55 24s1 15 -> 26/27 and 25nov 14 -> 25/26 with (d); s24 24s1 10 -> 24/27.
  - **Where (d) fails:** 25night ip18 23:03 and 23:08 (votes: 4F at 22:59 while still on 5F, 5F at 23:03, 4F at 23:08 and 23:17, so H = 2 switches at 23:17); a56 on 25night: the vote says 5F at 11 of its 13 4F stops (the a56 night magnetometer offset of entry 72), so it never switches.
  - **Decision rule** (`out/decision.json`): iPhone: R1 (+9 on 25night), c2 (+8) and (d) (+10) lose no stop that R0 gets right on 25night_tian, 26tian and 25spots; (a) loses 4, c1 gains 0. Phones without a barometer: (d) 137 vs 83 of 159 on the tuning a55/a56/s24 units but 19 vs 19 of 32 on the held-out a56 unit, so neither (a) (17 vs 19) nor (d) passes.
- **Outcome:**
  - Success for the iPhone: (d) (R0 unchanged when pressure is present and continuous; the magnetic vote only when pressure is missing or right after a gap longer than 15 min) gets 30/32 on the 25-night ip18 session where the app got 20/32, keeps every complete-pressure held-out stop, and, unlike R1 and c2, keeps the 4 weather stops of 24 s1.
  - Failure for the phones without a barometer: no candidate is shown to help on held-out data, because the only held-out no-barometer unit (a56, 25 night) has a faulty magnetometer.
  - (b) could not be tested: the data has no recording across a floor change.
  - c1 as asked ("keep the floor, re-anchor") is what the app already does; tuning the gap picked "never reset", which breaks the 24 s1 weather case and does not fix 25 night (the 4F reading after the gap was less than 0.40 hPa above the 5F reference).
- **What changed because of it:** a pre-registered, scored proposal for the app's floor rule (`phase2/new/floor/README.md` section 5: (d) with k = 15, P = 0.7, H = 2, M = 3). Nothing was wired into the app; that is a team decision.
- **Links:** `phase2/new/floor/` (`PREREGISTRATION.md`, `README.md`, `floorlib.py`, `rules.py`, `baseline.py`, `candidates.py`, `out/`); pre-registration commit a884c97; branch `feat/floor-robust`.

## 75. 26 Nov: request matching scored with Gemini (gemini-3.5-flash-lite, free tier) on the 128 queries; call pacing added

- **What we tried:** the user asked for LLM search and a Gemini key now exists. Scored the request matcher with Gemini: `gemini` (the LLM alone) and `hybrid_gemini` (keyword -> fuzzy -> Gemini), on all 128 queries of `test_queries.csv`, with the same frozen prompt (v1) and place list as the Claude Haiku `llm` method. First added a `--sleep-s` option to `evaluate.py` so the free-tier limit (about 15 requests/min) is respected.
- **Why:** entry 71 built the Gemini backend but no real call had been made; the team needed Gemini's real accuracy, latency and call count next to the Claude numbers before deciding what the app should call.
- **How:** in `phase2/request-matching`: `evaluate.py --method gemini --sleep-s 4.5 --max-calls 150`, then `--method hybrid_gemini` (all its LLM answers were already cached). `--sleep-s S` (default 0) waits so that two REAL calls are at least S seconds apart; cached answers are never delayed; on an error (429, timeout, key) the run still stops, the answers so far stay in `llm_cache_gemini_v1.json` and the same command resumes. `gemini_results.json` also records `api_calls_in_last_run` and `sleep_s` per method. The key was read from the main tree's gitignored `.env` through the shell environment for the one command (never copied, never printed). Run 1 (default `GEMINI_TIMEOUT_S` = 8 s) stopped after 101 calls because one reply took longer than 8 s; run 2 with `GEMINI_TIMEOUT_S=30` resumed from the cache and made the remaining 28 calls. Tests: `test_gemini.py` gained `test_pacing_and_resume_after_429` (fake HTTP layer: gap before the 2nd and 3rd call only, the 429 stops the run without a retry, the rerun makes only the 4 missing calls with 3 sleeps, a fully cached rerun makes 0 calls and 0 sleeps) and `test_default_no_sleep`. The Gemini run is not a tuning step for either LLM: the prompt was not changed, so the dev/test split is not used to tune Gemini.
- **Result:** (from `phase2/request-matching/out/gemini_results.json` and `results.json`; model reported by the API: `gemini-3.5-flash-lite`). Correct of 128: keyword 86, fuzzy_keyword 95, embed 96, hybrid (Claude Haiku) 115, llm (Claude Haiku) 125, hybrid_gemini 115, gemini 124. gemini: easy 23/23, hard dev 52/54, hard test 49/51, test 60/62, 0 invalid answers, "none" precision 26/30 and recall 26/26, supported requests wrongly rejected 4 of 102 (Claude llm: 3). Its 4 misses are all `NONE` answers: q030 "where do I go for my class", q031 "I'm late for my lecture" (both `tag:lecture`), q039 "which way out to the MTR" (`tag:exit`), q124 "where do I find the Y5 rooms". hybrid_gemini: the same 13 queries wrong as Claude's hybrid (compared with `diff` on the two failure files), all of them answered by the keyword or fuzzy stage before any LLM call. Latency (gemini, 128 calls): median 2710.5 ms, mean 3370.266, p95 6350, max 8409 (Claude CLI: median 5309.5, p95 7442, max 10960); hybrid_gemini when the LLM is called (42 of 128): median 2407.356 ms (Claude hybrid 5285.323). Calls: 129 real API calls in total (101 + 28), 0 for hybrid_gemini; one timeout, no HTTP 429. Tests: `python -m unittest test_gemini` 15 OK (was 13).
- **Outcome:** success. Gemini Flash-Lite is one query behind Claude Haiku on this set (124 vs 125) at about half the latency; both reject every NONE request (26/26). Failures are not label problems for the 13 hybrid misses (the local rules answer wrongly, e.g. `lecture theatre 3a` -> lt2a, `toilet for people with disabilities` -> male_toilet; they never reach the LLM). Possible label questions for the team in `test_queries.csv`: q012 "pintu keluar" expects `entrance_exit` but the matcher gives `exit_5` (the starter label is one reading of "exit door"); q039 "which way out to the MTR" is marked `tag:exit` in the note although the MTR route is not in our data, and q124 "the Y5 rooms" is plural and could be answered with any Y5 room or declined; q030/q031 are lenient `tag:lecture` rows that both LLMs sometimes decline. These are the agent-drafted queries: the team decides.
- **What changed because of it:** the README of `request-matching` has a Gemini results section (tables for all methods and by category); the Gemini answer cache is committed (queries and place-ID answers only). The `--sleep-s 4.5` flag is the recommended way to run it on the free tier. The app's 8 s `GEMINI_TIMEOUT_S` default is close to the tail of the latency (max 8409 ms, one timeout in 129 calls): the app then falls back to the offline answer, as designed.
- **Links:** `phase2/request-matching/README.md` ("Gemini results"), `evaluate.py`, `test_gemini.py`, `llm_cache_gemini_v1.json`, `out/gemini_results.json`, `out/gemini_failures_gemini.csv`, `out/gemini_failures_hybrid_gemini.csv`; entries 59, 64, 71; branch `feat/gemini-eval` (the commit that adds this entry).

## 76. 26 Nov: connector-gated floor rules ("entry/exit points"): the floor may only switch right after a lift/escalator landing; pre-registered, scored on held-out sessions; no gain, app not changed

- **What we tried:** the user's idea (26 Nov): "for escalator or lift changes, we have the entry and exit points, so detect from there." A floor change can only happen through a connector (lift/stairs, or the one-way escalators: down 5F -> 4F, up 4F -> 5F), so the floor may switch only right after the walker was at a connector landing, in the direction that connector allows; floor evidence elsewhere is ignored. Tested as a gate on the app rule R0, on R0 without its 15-min reset (eR0n), on the day-3 rule R1 and on entry 74's rule (d), with the previous place taken from the true labels (upper bound), from the day-3 fused kNN, or from the upload app's own place model (what the app could do live). Also: do the walk recordings that start or end at a connector show the change, and does pressure across consecutive recordings bracket it?
- **Why:** entry 74 found motion-based ride detection untestable (no recording spans a floor change), and its rule (d) needs a magnetic vote the app does not have; a connector gate would use only what the app already computes (the place guess) and should also stop weather-drift false switches.
- **How:**
  - Worktree `ge2340-landing`, branch `feat/landing-floor`; the gitignored clean trees and the day-3 feature cache were copied from the main tree, nothing rebuilt. `baseline.py` and `candidates.py` rerun first: `out/` unchanged, so entry 74 reproduces.
  - Pre-registration `phase2/new/floor/PREREGISTRATION_landing.md`, committed in 0e8ef6a **before any gated rule was run**: connector kinds and directions, the gate (a touch of an allowed kind at most T min before, the arrival landing itself included), the three gate sources, the gated rules, the grid (T in {5, 10, 20, 30, 45, 60} min; theta in {0.30, 0.40} for eR0n), tie-breaks, and the decision rule (entry 74's rule, plus: the app is changed only for an app-model-gated candidate that also loses no R0-right stop on any held-out unit). It lists what had been looked at before (the place sequences around each change, and the day-3 predictions at connector stops, where Dicta's 25-night lift stops were already seen to be misrecognised).
  - `landing_rules.py` (gate + gated rules; with the gate always open each one equals R0, R1 or (d), asserted on every unit) and `landing.py` (app-model predictions cached in `out/landing_pred_app.json`, tuning `out/landing_tuning.json`, frozen `out/landing_params.json`, scores `out/landing_scores.json` / `out/landing_table.csv`, `out/transitions.json`, `out/landing_gate_stats.json`, `out/landing_decision.json`, `out/fig_landing_scores.png`, `out/fig_landing_25night.png`). Write-up: `phase2/new/floor/README.md` section 7.
  - A first run of the descriptive tables asked the app model on the true floor of the arrival recording instead of the floor shown before it; fixed before the WIP commit (the scored rules always used the shown floor).
- **Result:**
  - **Frozen** (`out/landing_params.json`): T = 10 min for eR0, eR0n and ed with the true gate, 20 for eR1-true; 20 / 20 / 30 / 30 min (eR0 / eR0n / eR1 / ed) with the day-3 predictions; 20 / 45 / 60 / 45 min with the app model; theta 0.30 for eR0n with either prediction. Tuning sums (`out/landing_tuning.json`) against the ungated rule: eR0 280 (true) / 286 (day-3) vs 273; ed 374 / 358 vs 359 of 401; app-model scope (no 23 Nov): eR0 211, ed 276 vs 205 and 276 of 317.
  - **Held-out stops on the right floor** (`out/landing_table.csv`): 25night ip18: R0 20, R1 29, (d) 30 of 32; every pressure-only gated rule 20; ed-true 27, ed with either prediction 20. 25night_tian ip16: 23/23 for R0, but 14/23 for all four rules gated by the day-3 predictions and for eR0 gated by the app model. 26tian 25/25 for every rule. 25spots ip16 4/6 for every gated rule except ed-pred_d3 5/6. 25night a56 19/32 for every rule.
  - **Decision** (`out/landing_decision.json`): no app-model or day-3-gated candidate passes (all gain 0 on 25night ip18); only ed-true passes the iPhone rule (+7) and it is the upper bound, 3 below (d), and loses 4 R0-right stops on 25spots s24. No-barometer phones: none passes (a56 19 vs 19); tuning a55 + a56 + s24: ed-true 150, ed-pred_app 137, ed-pred_d3 136 vs (d) 137 and 83 kept-floor, of 159. `app.wire` = null.
  - **Why** (`out/transitions.json`, `out/landing_gate_stats.json`): the gate opened for 20 of 22 labelled changes with the true places but for 13 of 22 (day-3) and 11 of 21 (app model); the app model never names the arrival landing (0 of 21), being asked among the places of the old floor. At top-1 the day-3 kNN names the right connector kind at 37 of 101 connector stops (and a connector at 57 of 310 other stops), the app model 32 of 88 (44 of 281). A missed gate costs the rest of the floor (the jump joins the reference): 25night_tian 9 stops. With the true places there are 243 touches, 205 of them not followed by a change. The 25-night dropout is not a gating problem: pressure comes back 33.2 min after the change with a 0.379 hPa step.
  - **What the gate did fix:** R1's weather switch after the 24 s1 break: eR1 with any gate 27/27 (R1 23/27) on 24s1 ip18 (tuning unit). R0 already handles that case with its reset.
  - **Entry/exit points in the recordings** (`out/transitions.json`): 61 iPhone walks with a connector endpoint and pressure; the largest change inside one is 0.041 hPa (none above 0.40), so no walk with pressure spans a change. Pressure across consecutive recordings brackets every iPhone change with pressure on both sides: +0.684 (23nov), +0.424 (24s1), +0.565 (24s2), +0.651 (25nov), +0.575 (25night_tian), +0.625 (26tian), -0.67 and +0.614 (25night 24:15, 24:39), +0.591 (25spots 17:49) hPa; the exceptions are the long gaps (25night 23:03 +0.379, 25spots 20:22 +0.291). The only recording across a change is the 23-Nov stairs walk ACC23 (18:34:17 to 18:34:47, no barometer file), bracketed by BM26 1015.014 and BM27 1015.698 hPa (+0.684).
- **Outcome:** failure for the idea as pre-registered: no gated rule beats R0 on held-out data, and the realistic gates (day-3 or app place guess) lose 9 stops on a complete-pressure session because the connectors are too often not recognised. The true-place gate shows the idea is sound in principle (it removes no real change in 20 of 22 cases and stops R1's weather switch), but the place guess at the lift and escalators is not good enough to drive it. Entry 74's (d) remains the best candidate for the missing-pressure case.
- **What changed because of it:** nothing in the app (decision rule: no eligible candidate passed). New offline files in `phase2/new/floor/` only.
- **Links:** `phase2/new/floor/PREREGISTRATION_landing.md`, `landing_rules.py`, `landing.py`, `README.md` section 7, `out/landing_*`, `out/transitions.json`, `out/fig_landing_*.png`; pre-registration commit 0e8ef6a, WIP commit e59b302; branch `feat/landing-floor`.

## 77. 26 Nov: a56 night offset: keep, exclude or recalibrate (offline comparison, default config unchanged)

- **What we tried:** give the team numbers for the a56 decision of entry 72 (Chris's SM-A566B read a median |B| of 110.9 uT on the night run against 40 to 45 uT on the other days). Six options, each run through the unchanged day-3 pipeline (`evaluate.py` + `fused_knn.py`, default and split20) on main with `26tian` (entry 73): **A** keep; **B** exclude the a56 night takes; **C** a hard-iron correction with the same rule for every phone and session; **C1** (C' in the request) the same correction on the a56 night takes only; **C2** / **C21** a time-local version of C for every phone / for the a56 night only. Also: is the a56 offset constant over the night, and should anyone redo the night route?
- **Why:** the night takes are kept in `config.json` (entry 72), score 2/32 and pull a56 down; the team has to choose keep, exclude or recalibrate, and the choice changes the headline numbers.
- **How:**
  - New `phase2/new/day3/a56_offset.py` (+ `a56_offset.sh`, about 2 h with 12 runs in parallel, one core each) writes only `phase2/new/day3/out-a56/`; `config.json`, `out/` and `out-split20/` are not touched. It swaps `data.load()` and `data.OUT` in-process, so the evaluation code is the committed code.
  - B uses the existing `exclude_sources` mechanism in a derived config (`out-a56/config_B.json`, `exclude_a56_night.csv`: the a56 recordings of `25night`).
  - Correction rule, fixed before any accuracy was computed: a hard-iron offset is a constant vector in the phone's frame; turning a flat phone moves the true field on a circle around it. Every stop take was recorded flat (gravity along z: `flat_check.csv`, smallest g_z 0.973), so the offset in x/y = the centre of a circle fitted to the take-mean x/y of all stop takes of that phone in that session (algebraic start, geometric least squares, takes with a residual over 3 robust sd dropped). Guards: at least 10 takes kept, radius 20 to 60 uT, no angular gap over 180 deg, else no correction. z is not corrected (turning a flat phone says nothing about it). No labels are used. Subtracting each take's own mean was rejected: a still 60 s take's mean IS its fingerprint. The time-local rule fits the takes within 30 min of each take (at least 8), falling back to the session fit.
  - The offset is subtracted from the raw magnetometer samples of every stop take before the 5 s windows, so it changes x/y/z, |B| (mean, sd, p10, p90) and tilt (B_vertical/B_horizontal use the corrected mean field). Wi-Fi and the barometer are untouched; walks are not corrected (only the walk-endpoint test uses them, not part of this comparison).
  - Commands: `sh phase2/new/day3/a56_offset.sh` (= `a56_offset.py fields`, `prepare`, `run <option> <default|--split20>` x 12, `report`), then `a56_offset.py xlsx`.
- **Result:**
  - **Offset fits** (`out-a56/fits.csv`, `B_before_after.csv`): a56 night centre (-32.5, -84.6) uT, radius 38.0 uT, close to entry 72's offset against the first 25-Nov run (-35.4 / -77.6, `field_check.csv`). Every other phone and session: centre within 15 uT of zero except a55 24s1 (13.5, 15.0), a55 25nov (-1.8, -32.4) and s24 25spots (-15.3, -29.5); s24 24s1 and ip16 25spots failed the guards (no correction). Radii of the fits that passed: 32.9 to 47.0 uT.
  - **|B| before -> after** (median over takes): a56 night 111.4 -> 50.6 uT (C/C1) and 47.6 uT (C2/C21); a56 on other days 40.5 and 45.2. Stop takes over 100 uT in the a56 night: 21 -> 1. `results.json` `magnetic_check` stops over 100 uT: 24 (A) -> 9 or 10 (every other option).
  - **Offset over the night** (`night_drift.json`, `night_timeseries_takes.csv`, `fig_a56_night_offset.png`): not constant. Median take offset against the same place in the first 25-Nov run (x/y/z, uT): -29.8 / -107.4 / 1.6 before 22:59 (sd 30.5 / 31.6 / 10.5) and -41.6 / -68.8 / -4.4 from 23:00 (sd 24.4 / 19.5 / 7.0); ip18 on the same walk 3.3 / -2.1 / 0.6. At 22:59 (y5302_back) both phones saw a strong field (ip18 take |B| 284.3 uT), and within that walk + stop the a56 y field went from about -115 to +78 uT (`night_trace_1s.csv`); after it the offset sits in a second regime. The time-local fits give x -44.2 to -32.5 and y -125.2 to -71.1 uT over the night; 13 of the 32 takes fell back to the session fit.
  - **Leave one session out** (learned-weight fused kNN, `out-a56/comparison.csv`; "same samples" = every test sample except the a56 night, identical in every option):

    | option | default | same 379 takes | a56 | a56 night | split20 | same 1092 pieces | a56 | a56 night |
    |---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
    | A | 179/411 (43.6%) | 177/379 | 30/85 | 2/32 | 553/1183 (46.7%) | 541/1092 | 94/246 | 12/91 |
    | B | 190/379 (50.1%) | 190/379 | 30/53 | excluded | 526/1092 (48.2%) | 526/1092 | 85/155 | excluded |
    | C | 197/411 (47.9%) | 183/379 | 43/85 | 14/32 | 573/1180 (48.6%) | 531/1089 | 125/246 | 42/91 |
    | C1 | 194/411 (47.2%) | 183/379 | 42/85 | 11/32 | 614/1183 (51.9%) | 583/1092 | 114/246 | 31/91 |
    | C2 | 178/411 (43.3%) | 166/379 | 44/85 | 12/32 | 524/1181 (44.4%) | 486/1090 | 128/246 | 38/91 |
    | C21 | 201/411 (48.9%) | 185/379 | 46/85 | 16/32 | 599/1183 (50.6%) | 556/1092 | 137/246 | 43/91 |
  - Per phone, default (A -> B / C / C1 / C2 / C21): ip18 58/85 -> 59 / 53 / 58 / 46 / 59; ip16 61/122 -> 63 / 66 / 61 / 56 / 64; s24 17/66 -> 17 / 18 / 17 / 19 / 19; a55 13/53 -> 21 / 17 / 16 / 13 / 13. Split20: ip18 174/253 -> 175 / 161 / 180 / 134 / 179; ip16 189/340 -> 183 / 191 / 212 / 170 / 185; s24 60/198 -> 47 / 58 / 71 / 55 / 62; a55 36/146 -> 36 / 38 / 37 / 37 / 36.
  - a56 on its other sessions (24s1 + 25nov): default 28/53 (A), 30 (B), 29 (C), 31 (C1), 32 (C2), 30 (C21); split20 82/155 (A), 85, 83, 83, 90, 94.
  - **a56 night per place** (default, `a56_night_per_place_wide.csv`): A gets lt2a 1/1 and sr9 1/2. C21 gets canteen, entrance_exit, escalator_down_4F, exit_5, exit_10_4F, lt1b, lt2a, lt2b, male_toilet, y4502 (1/1 each), sr9 2/2, y5401 2/2, exit_10 1/2, lift_stairs 1/2. Never right in any option or variant: disabled_toilet, disabled_toilet_4F, exit_5_4F, female_toilet, lt1a, y5302.
  - **Leave 25 Nov out** (`results.json`, |B|+xyz+tilt + baro floor, kNN k=5; the a56 night is only in the test set): a56 all phones 16/58 (A) -> 22/58 (C1), 24/58 (C, C21); B 13/26 (night removed). On the night part (58 minus the 25nov 26 takes at 13 right): 3/32 -> 9/32 (C1), 11/32 (C21). Same handset 12/58 -> 18 (C1), 17 (C21). Split20 all phones 53/166 -> 72 (C1), 76 (C21).
  - **Reproduction:** the input tables of option A equal `data.load()` (W, V, O, E, A identical) and A's `results.json` `stop_results` equal the official ones in both variants. The fused kNN does not reproduce the committed `out/` exactly: A gives 179/411 default (official 178/411) and 553/1183 split20 (= official total, but other per-sample predictions); 18 of 23 default folds and 17 of 23 split20 folds chose the same weights and k as the official run, inner accuracies within 0.009 and 0.005 (`comparison.json`). In the leave-one-session-out scheme only the 25night fold differs (k 3 and 23/64 here, k 9 and 22/64 official). A second run of A with normal multi-threading (`out-a56/A_mt*`) equals the one-thread run in all 23 folds, so the rerun here is deterministic; the committed official run came from another process state that this rerun does not reproduce (open question). Every option here was run the same way, so they compare with each other; differences against the official files of 1 take are this effect.
- **Outcome:**
  - A label-free circle fit finds the a56 night offset (it agrees with the offset measured against the first run) and brings its |B| back to 48 to 51 uT, but the offset drifts, so the corrected takes stay worse than the a56's normal takes: default 11 to 16 of 32 against 28 to 32 of 53 on its other sessions.
  - Correcting every phone (C, C2) hurts ip18 (default 58 -> 53 and 46; split20 174 -> 161 and 134): do not apply the correction to phones without a fault.
  - On the same 379 takes / 1092 pieces the other phones move both ways with the option (B +13 default but -15 split20; C1 +6 / +42; C21 +8 / +15). The rerun of A alone differs from the official run by 1 take and in 5 of 23 folds, so changes of a few percent on the other phones are mostly the weight tuning, not the a56 choice.
  - **Recommendation (team to decide; `config.json` default left unchanged):** quote B (exclude the a56 night takes with `exclude_sources`) for the headline numbers: it is the simplest rule to defend (a sensor fault found by a label-free |B| check, no fitted correction in the test session), and it removes the 2/32 takes without touching the data. Report C1 (one offset per phone and session, a56 night only) as the evidence that the fault is a hard-iron offset that a fit without labels partly repairs (2/32 -> 11/32 default, 12/91 -> 31/91 split20). Do not use C or C2. C21 scored highest on the a56 night (16/32, 43/91) but it was chosen after seeing that the offset drifts and 13 of its 32 fits fell back, so treat it as exploratory.
  - **Redo question (team chat 26 Nov):** "Chris forgot the barometer" is not the cause: the A56 has never logged pressure in any session (entry 87: 0/55, 0/54, 0/58 raw ZIPs; here also 0/27, 0/26, 0/32 a56 stops with `n_baro` > 0 in the manifests), and day 3 reads pressure only from the iPhone and S24 slots. The a56 problem is its magnetometer. A full a56 redo is worth it only with a check first: at one known spot compare the a56 |B| with the ip18 (they should both read about 35 to 45 uT, `field_check.csv`), take off any magnetic case or holder, and do not record if the a56 reads far higher. An ip18 redo of floor 5 is not needed for the fingerprints (ip18 night offset 3.3 / -2.1 / 0.6 uT, 21/32 night takes right in every option except C/C2); its only gap is pressure on 13 night stops (10 on 5F, `build_summary.json`), which day-3 step tracking already gets 29/32 on. Priority: a checked a56 redo > ip18 floor 5.
- **What changed because of it:**
  - New `phase2/new/day3/a56_offset.py`, `a56_offset.sh`, `out-a56/` (per option and variant: `fused_knn.json`, `fused_model.json`, `fused_sample_predictions.csv`, `summary_table.csv`; `results.json` and window predictions are regenerable and gitignored); `day3/README.md` section o.
  - New tab "2026-11-26 a56 options" appended to `out/phone_place_performance.xlsx` (the 16 earlier tabs checked unchanged); copied to Downloads as `WalkSense phone_place_performance 2026-11-26b.xlsx`, the older copy kept.
  - `results-pack/timeline_facts.json`: the night run has its own line (entries 72, 77). `build.py` rebuilt: 1768 facts, 10 figures, 0 missing items; the committed pack had last been built before `26tian` and the Gemini results landed on main, so it now also carries those (it reads the official `out/`, not `out-a56/`).
  - No change to `config.json`, `out/`, `out-split20/` or the upload app.
- **Links:** `phase2/new/day3/a56_offset.py`, `a56_offset.sh`, `out-a56/` (`comparison.csv/.json`, `a56_night_per_place*.csv`, `fits.csv`, `offsets.csv`, `B_before_after.csv`, `flat_check.csv`, `night_drift.json`, `night_timeseries_takes.csv`, `night_trace_1s.csv`, `fig_a56_night_offset.png`); `phase2/new/results-pack/timeline_facts.json`; branch `feat/a56-offset` commits 4d044e6, f1ba0ef, d608002 (merge of main), 54f72ab and the commit that completes this entry.

## 78. 26 Nov: route planner in the upload app: shortest path A -> B lit on the map, lift / stairs / escalator as separate connectors, step-free mode and toilet gender filter

- **What we tried:** the user's request (26 Nov): "if someone wants to go from B to E, light up the suggested path by calculating the lowest distance (we have B->C, C->D, D->E on the map); if the floor changes, suggest the closest lift or escalator; a disabled mode with no stairs or escalators, only lifts, and disabled toilets only; filter toilets by gender". Build it into Tian's upload app: pick a start and a destination (lists, search or a tap on the plan), draw the cheapest path with the length of each leg, the total metres and an estimated time, model each connector separately, a Step-free mode and a Male / Female / Any toilet filter.
- **Why:** entry 71 drew an A* route only for a searched place, treated the lift and the stairs as one edge ("take the stairs (or the lift)"), and had no accessibility or toilet logic.
- **How:**
  - `phase2/new/routing/route.py`: `Graph(profile=...)`. `profile=None` = graph.json as it is (so `route()`, `check_astar.py`, `evaluate.py`, `llm_baseline.py` and their outputs are unchanged). `standard` / `accessible`: `connector_edges()` splits each `lift_stairs` edge into a stairs edge (graph.json's 30.0 s and 28.6 m) and a lift edge (`LIFT_WAIT_S` 30 s + `LIFT_RIDE_S` 12 s per floor = 42.0 s, 0 m walked; both GUESSES, nobody timed the lift) between the same landings, both directions; escalators stay one way (20 s, graph.json guess). Only the cheapest allowed parallel edge is searched; the instructions still name the other ("the lift is next to them"). `accessible` keeps walk + lift only. `plan(start, goal, mode)` -> path, legs (kind, metres, seconds), connectors, metres, seconds, instructions. `nearest_toilet(start, mode, gender, floor, accessible_only)`: `TOILETS` table (5F male, 5F female, 5F male disabled, 4F mixed disabled; names from `day3/config.json` `place_display`), Male -> male + mixed, Female -> female + mixed, Step-free -> disabled only, ranked by route cost; no fit -> `choice = None` and a message listing every toilet with its reason. `python route.py samples` -> `routing/out/planner_samples.json`.
  - `phase2/new/upload/search.py`: `plan_route()`, `nearest()`, `route_payload()`, `resolve_start()`; `search(q, from, tracked, mode, gender)`: a toilet request (toilet words, or a match that is a toilet) goes to `nearest()`; words in the question win over the filter (gender words, "disabled"/"accessible"/"wheelchair", "4F"/"5F"); a Gemini pick that differs is kept as `matched_place` and its sentence replaced. `server.py`: `GET /api/route?from=&to=&mode=`, `GET /api/nearest?type=toilet&from=&mode=&gender=`, mode/gender on `/api/search`, 400 for a bad value. No change to `walksense.py`, `live.py` or `phase2/new/floor/`.
  - `index.html` + `map/floorplan.js`: From / To lists under the search box; Standard / Step-free and Any WC / Male / Female chips and a "Nearest toilet" button in the sticky top bar (screen edge, no popup); places on the plan are tappable (invisible bigger touch target; a drag does not count as a tap) with a "Tap a place to set To / From" switch under the map; orange metres on each walked leg (the grey corridor label under it is hidden), orange connector tags (`Lift ▼ 4F`, `Lift from 5F`), card with metres on foot, time, floor change and its cost, step list, other fitting toilets and the ones left out. Mode and filter remembered in `localStorage` (try/catch). In live mode with From empty, the route is re-run when a new place lights up.
  - Every graph edge already has a length (8 measured, 23 steps, 3 guesses), so nothing was derived from `floorplan.json`.
- **Result** (`phase2/new/routing/out/planner_samples.json`):
  - Y5-302 -> Female toilet (5F): Y5-302 -> study rooms 13.9 m -> Y5-202 10.6 m -> Female toilet 10.7 m (all measured) = 35.2 m, 27.1 s, same in both modes.
  - Canteen -> LT1a: Standard 36.6 m, 48.2 s by the down escalator; Step-free 65.0 m, 92.0 s by the lift. Y5-302 -> LT2B: 28.2 m, 41.7 s (escalator) vs 57.8 m, 86.5 s (lift). LT1a -> Canteen: 28.0 m, 41.5 s (up escalator) vs 65.0 m, 92.0 s (lift). Lift landing 5F -> 4F: Standard stairs 30.0 s, Step-free lift 42.0 s. 5F up escalator -> 4F down escalator: Standard walks to the down escalator first (10.3 m, 27.9 s).
  - Over the 336 ordered cross-floor pairs: Standard uses the escalator for 251 and the stairs for 85, never the lift (lift 42 s > stairs 30 s at the same landing); Step-free uses the lift for all 336.
  - Toilets from 8 starts (4 per floor) x 2 modes x 3 genders, e.g. canteen: Any / Male -> Male disabled toilet 14.3 m (male toilet 14.7 m), Female -> Female toilet 16.0 m; Step-free + Female -> Mixed disabled toilet (4F) 30.5 m by the lift; every 4F start -> the 4F mixed disabled toilet in every mode and gender. No-option example: Step-free + Female + "5F" -> "No disabled female toilet on 5F ...".
  - Tests: `routing/test_route.py` 13 OK (4 old + directional escalators in every mode, Step-free never uses stairs/escalators over all pairs and always the lift across floors, lift chosen when stairs excluded, Standard costs = old `route()` costs and A* = Dijkstra in both modes, toilet table = graph toilets, toilet choice under every mode x gender from 8 starts checked against an independent Dijkstra, no-option cases). `upload/test_search.py` 16 OK (10 old + toilet requests under the filter, no-option message, Gemini pick post-processed, step-free search route, `/api/route` and `/api/nearest` through a real server on a free port). `test_search` + `test_walksense` + `test_live` together: 23 OK.
  - Screenshots (headless Chromium 1234 against `server.Handler` on port 8020, place models not trained because the worktree has no sensor CSVs): `phase2/new/upload/screenshots/v4-*.png`, 10 files; no console errors; page width = viewport width at 390 px (dark and light) and 520 px.
- **Outcome:** success for the planner, the modes, the toilet filter and the page, offline. The connector times are guesses except the one stairs walk, so which connector Standard prefers can change once the lift and escalators are timed (the lift would beat the stairs only with a wait under 18 s). Not tested on a phone on site or with a real Gemini key.
- **What changed because of it:** the app routes between any two of the 26 places, names the lift / stairs / escalator it uses, has a Step-free mode and a toilet filter, and exposes `/api/route` and `/api/nearest`. `routing/README.md` section "Route planner" and `upload/README.md` section "Route planner" document the costs and the API.
- **Links:** `phase2/new/routing/route.py`, `test_route.py`, `README.md`, `out/planner_samples.json`; `phase2/new/upload/search.py`, `server.py`, `index.html`, `map/floorplan.js`, `test_search.py`, `README.md`, `screenshots/v4-*.png`; entries 56 to 59 (routing), 70, 71; branch `feat/route-planner` (WIP commits c884b79, 077aa37, dda5603, merge of main 3e4bdbc, and the commit that completes this entry).
