## 79. 25 to 26 Nov: getting the recordings out of WhatsApp: exports silently left out teammates' files; fixed by per-person exports, a GitHub push and an inventory of manual downloads

- **What we tried:** collect every phyphox ZIP of the 25-Nov afternoon run (entries 28, 42), the 25-Nov night run (entry 72) and Tian's 26-Nov morning pass (entry 73) from the team WhatsApp group onto the laptop, without losing or doubling any file.
- **Why:** all recordings are shared by posting the ZIPs in the group chat; the repo only gets what reaches the laptop.
- **How and what happened, in order:**
  - **First export (25 Nov evening), text only.** The WhatsApp Desktop "Export chat" on the laptop has no "include media" option. The first export held the chat text with every ZIP, both WiGLE logs and Tian's `WalkSense ranking by phone.xlsx` written as omitted.
  - **Second export, partial.** After files were opened in the chat on the laptop, the next export carried only those files: 52 usable files (the round 17:35 to 18:17, Michael's 11 S24 spots without names, one WiGLE log), and 73 media messages still omitted (Benedicta 35, Chris 37, Tian 1; `phase2/clean-data/2026-11-25-spots/raw/missing_from_export.csv`, entry 42). Cause, found then: **an export only contains media that this device has downloaded**; the laptop had never downloaded most teammates' files.
  - **Fix 1: the collector pushes her own set.** Dicta pushed her complete 25-Nov dataset to GitHub (`2cb50d8`, entry 28), which replaced the partial export (entries 47 to 49); the A55 raw file that had also been sent by e-mail was inside her push (`attachments.zip`), so nothing was taken from e-mail. 36 of our 52 export files were byte-identical to hers (entries 49, 68).
  - **Night run, three more laptop exports, no new data.** Export "(1)" (26 Nov, early morning) held only 2 recordings not yet in the repo (Michael's S24 takes at 20:22 to 20:24); the whole night run (25 Nov about 21:40 to 26 Nov 00:40) was again omitted. Export "(2)" was the same chat with export (1) re-posted into the group as a file, and export "(3)" contained (2) and (1) re-posted. Re-exporting from the same device cannot fix the omission.
  - **Fix 2: each person exports from their own phone.** Dicta's export from her own iPhone contained her own 59 night recordings (an export carries what the exporter sent and downloaded); its other files were byte-identical to files already in the repo and were skipped by hash (entry 72, `ingest_export.py`).
  - **Fix 3: manual downloads, checked by an inventory.** Tian's and Chris's files were downloaded one by one from WhatsApp Desktop as loose files. Because nobody could tell whether files had been skipped or clicked twice, the loose files were checked against the chat's own list of what each person sent (file name and send time, read from the export's text, not kept). The inventory found that all 8 of Chris's extra takes at the weak places (00:16 to 00:40, the last files of his batch, after his WiGLE log) had not been downloaded, and that 2 of his files had been downloaded twice. The 8 were downloaded, and every file was then hashed on ingest: 2 byte-duplicates skipped, 169 recordings + 1 WiGLE log kept (`2026-11-25-night/build_summary.json`, entry 72; the night README lists Chris's 32 stops as 24 loop + 8 extra).
  - **Tian's morning pass (26 Nov):** his phone saved 51 of 52 files as "point <time>"; the labels came from his chat captions, matched one-to-one in order to the files (entry 73). The chat export was used only for that matching and is not in the repo.
  - **Near miss, the two S24 takes at 20:22 to 20:24:** a first look at their timing suggested they were taken with Tian's `y5401` and `y5302 back` stops. They had not been labelled yet; Dicta's export showed Michael's own names for them, "toilet cowo" (male toilet, 5F) and "esc turun lvl 5" (escalator down, 5F). They were labelled from those names (`2026-11-25-spots/labels_override.csv`, `label_source` = `user_filename`, entry 72); the timing guess was never written to any file. The same mistake had happened once already: the 3 timing guesses of entry 42 were all wrong (entry 69).
- **Result:** no recording of the night run is known to be missing from the downloads (the night README lists what was never recorded: the 5F escalator stops of Dicta and Chris, two walks and Tian's 4F exit 5 door). No chat text was committed (`.gitignore`, `CLAUDE.md`).
- **Outcome:** success in the end, after several failed exports. The failure was silent: an export looks complete and only the omitted lines in the text show what is missing.
- **What changed because of it:** the team rule is now (a) each collector exports from their own phone or pushes their raw ZIPs to GitHub, (b) loose downloads are checked against the sender's list before ingest, (c) every file is hashed against the repo (`ingest_export.py`), (d) labels come from the collector's own names or captions, never from timing. The duplicate exports left in the laptop's Downloads folder are outside the repo and were not deleted (the user's decision).
- **Links:** entries 28, 42, 47 to 49, 68, 69, 72, 73; `phase2/clean-data/2026-11-25-spots/raw/missing_from_export.csv`, `labels_override.csv`, `README.md` ("Added 26 Nov"); `phase2/clean-data/2026-11-25-night/README.md`, `build_summary.json`, `scripts/ingest_export.py`; `phase2/clean-data/2026-11-26-tian/scripts/ingest_tian26.py`.

## 80. 25 to 26 Nov: worker runs that stopped on usage limits (HTTP 429) or network blocks (HTTP 403), and how they were resumed

- **What we tried:** run the analysis and build jobs as background workers (at most 5 at once, the user's rule from 25 Nov), each in its own git worktree and branch, and keep going when a run is cut off.
- **Why:** several long jobs (reruns of about 6 minutes each, ingest, app work) ran in parallel; any of them can be stopped by the account's usage limit or by the network.
- **How and what happened** (times from the session record; commit times from `git log`):
  - **25 Nov about 20:40, usage limit (HTTP 429, "5-hour limit").** Two workers stopped mid-run: the 25-Nov data + per-phone sheet job and the live-smoothing study. Their work so far was kept; after the user logged in again they were resumed with a message (not restarted) and finished: entries 43 to 45 (`ae96b0e` 20:37, `f8cf06d` and `321f6c1` 20:54) and 60 to 61 (`15e11ba` 20:58, `a51bc99` 21:17).
  - **25 Nov about 21:14, usage limit again** for the coordinating session; the automatic security review of a commit could not run (HTTP 429). It runs again on a later push.
  - **26 Nov about 00:39 (the coordinating session) and 02:26 / 02:36 (the Gemini-search worker), network block (HTTP 403, "request blocked before it reached the API: unsupported region, VPN or proxy").** The Gemini-search worker stopped twice, the first time before it had changed any file. Signing in again does not help with a 403; the run was restarted once the network allowed it and finished: commits `bfe5017` to `435e654` (02:39 to 02:45), merged as `6f54a80` (02:46, entry 71).
  - **26 Nov about 02:49, usage limit** during the night-run ingest (entry 72), with its config and pipeline changes not yet committed. It was resumed after the user logged in again (about 02:53) and finished: `5d51f45` (02:55) to `fcfd79b` (04:31), merged as `9e432e2` (04:32).
  - The private claude.ai pages that show the playground and the figure gallery had been published under another login; after the login changed they could not be updated and were republished as new pages. The repo copies (`phase2/new/day3/out*/knn_playground.html`, `phase2/new/results-pack/out/`) are the source of truth.
- **Result:** no work was lost and no job had to start from zero; every stopped job ended in a commit listed above.
- **Outcome:** success after interruptions. A worker that stops leaves uncommitted files in its worktree, so the "WIP" commits that the later jobs made after each step (e.g. `5d51f45`, `5758f0e`, `42020ad` on `feat/night-run`) made a resume cheap and checkable.
- **What changed because of it:** long jobs commit WIP steps on their branch; the Gemini evaluation got `--sleep-s` pacing and a resumable cache (entry 75); a 403 is treated as a network problem (VPN off), a 429 as a wait-and-resume.
- **Links:** entries 43 to 45, 60, 61, 71, 72, 75; commits above.

## 81. 26 Nov: FAILURE, the upload app would not start on main after the night run landed (`KeyError: 'mag'`): the cleaned sensor CSVs are gitignored

- **What we tried:** restart Tian's upload app (`phase2/new/upload/server.py --port 8000 --live <phone> --device iPhone19,2 --floor 5`) in the main tree right after merging `feat/night-run` (`9e432e2`, 26 Nov 04:32).
- **Why:** the app trains on the sessions in `ALL_SESSIONS` at start-up, which now included `25night` and `25night_tian` (entry 72).
- **How / Result:** start-up stopped in `walksense.build_models()` -> `PlaceModel(training_stops(...))` -> `stop_features` with `KeyError: 'mag'`: the stops of the new sessions had no magnetometer data in the main tree. Cause: the per-recording sensor CSVs under `phase2/clean-data/2026-11-25-night/{round,tian}/clean/floor4|floor5/` (and the new spot folders and `wifi/observations.csv` under `2026-11-25-spots/clean/`) are gitignored by design (`CLAUDE.md`: big CSVs are regenerated), so the merge brought the tables and the code but not the CSVs that the worker had built in its own worktree. `git status --ignored` in the worktree listed exactly those folders. Fix: the generated folders were copied from the worktree into the main tree (they are rebuilt byte for byte by `build_night.py`), then the app started and `/` and `/api/search` answered (HTTP 200).
- **Outcome:** fixed in a few minutes; nothing in git changed. The error message did not say "data not built", which cost time.
- **What changed because of it:** the night README and the advisor's note to teammates say: after pulling, run `python -I phase2/clean-data/2026-11-25-night/scripts/build_night.py` (or the day-3 `run_all.sh`, which runs it first) before starting the app. The floor study (entry 74) copied the same gitignored CSVs on purpose and said so. Not done: a clear start-up check in the app that names the missing folder and the build command (would be a code change; suggested, not made here).
- **Links:** `phase2/new/upload/walksense.py` (`stop_features`, `build_models`), `.gitignore`, `phase2/clean-data/2026-11-25-night/README.md` (layout; rebuild command), entries 72, 74.

## 82. 25 to 26 Nov: log numbers when parallel branches land (provisional numbers, renumbered on merge)

- **What we tried:** keep one gap-free, ordered `PROCESS-LOG.md` while up to five branches wrote entries at the same time.
- **Why:** every branch appends at the end of the same file, so two branches pick the same next number (first seen in entry 39, again in 48 and 63).
- **How:** each branch writes a provisional number (often far ahead, e.g. 80, 90, 100) and states it in its commit messages; whoever lands the branch gives it the next free number, updates the contents list and every "entry N" reference, and says so in the merge commit. A branch that stays open while main moves merges main back in and renumbers again.
- **Result** (from commit messages):
  - 25 Nov: routing 80 to 83 -> 52 to 55, request matching 70 to 73 -> 56 to 59, results pack 90 -> 62, smoothing 60 to 61 kept (entry 63).
  - 26 Nov: `feat/vector-map` 90 -> 70 (`916d231`, merge `ad62427`, note `9e1b9fa`); `feat/tian-morning` 74 -> 73 (`b6b53c8`); `feat/floor-robust` 76 -> 74 (`8fef028`); `feat/gemini-eval` 76 -> 75 (`fccc235`); `feat/a56-offset` (still open) 73 -> 74 (`f1ba0ef`) -> 75 (`54f72ab`) -> 76 (`6ee36d5`), merging main twice (`d608002`, `aff8a2b`).
  - Later on 26 Nov: `feat/landing-floor` 78 -> 76 (`e46596c`). Reserved at the time of writing: A56 offset 77 (76 on its branch), route planner 78 (77 on its branch, `feat/route-planner`), this gap audit 79 to 88 (`docs/project-story`). The numbers 77 to 88 are provisional until those branches land.
  - `feat/user-test` (provisional 100) never landed (entry 83).
- **Outcome:** success: entries 1 to 75 on main are gap-free. Cost: commit messages written before a merge carry old numbers (the mapping is in the merge commits and entries 39, 48, 63), and a long-lived branch had to be renumbered three times.
- **What changed because of it:** the lesson in entry 39 ("number log entries only when they land") is applied to every branch; three branches open at once is the practical limit for the log file.
- **Links:** entries 39, 48, 63; commits above.

- **What we tried:** a kit for the user tests promised in the proposal: a protocol (4 tasks on 5F/4F, with the app and with signs only, counterbalanced, 5 to 8 people), a phone form with a timer, the SUS questionnaire, a consent note and an analysis script, tested only on a clearly marked FAKE example dataset.

The worker had committed under a placeholder author name; the author was corrected to the repo owner before the branch was kept.

The branch is kept, unmerged and unpushed, in case it is wanted later; its numbers are FAKE and must never be quoted.

## 84. 25 to 26 Nov: decisions on search: LLM with a keyword -> fuzzy offline fallback, no RAG at this size, Gemini on the free tier

- **What we tried:** decide how the app turns a typed request into one of the 26 places, using the measured comparison (entries 57 to 59, 64) instead of opinion.
- **Why:** the proposal preferred an LLM with keywords as the offline fallback; the user questioned whether an LLM is needed at all for so few places.
- **How (the sequence of decisions, 25 Nov evening to 26 Nov morning):**
  1. The user asked why an LLM is needed when the search is "like Google Maps" with very few options. The first recommendation was keyword + fuzzy matching with the LLM only as a fallback, and no embeddings (they tied with keywords on the test split, 44/62 each, entry 59, and needed a large install).
  2. The user chose the LLM, because it answers questions like "where can I study?" or "where is the chemistry lab?"; the hybrid keyword -> fuzzy -> LLM was scored from the cached answers (entry 64: hybrid 56/62 on test, keyword 44/62, fuzzy keyword 49/62, LLM 60/62).
  3. The user pointed out that the map would not work without internet. The design kept an offline path: when there is no key, no internet, a timeout or a bad reply, the app answers with keyword -> fuzzy (entry 71).
  4. RAG: not used. The whole place list (26 lines: id, name, floor, tags, aliases) goes into every prompt (prompt grounding), so no retrieval step can miss the right place. RAG becomes useful only at campus scale (hundreds of rooms), with the tested embeddings as the retrieval step: a future-work item, not built.
  5. Provider: the user asked for everything to be ready so that he only pastes a Gemini key. The key lives in a gitignored `.env` at the repo root (`.env.example` committed; the user had written ".git", which was taken to mean `.env`). The free tier was accepted: pacing for the evaluation, offline fallback if a limit is hit in a demo, and a note that free-tier prompts may be used by the provider, so nothing personal should be typed into the search bar. The key check passed and the app was restarted with it (26 Nov morning); the key was never committed or printed (entries 71, 75).
- **Result:** Gemini scored 124/128 alone and hybrid_gemini 115/128 (entry 75), the same 13 hybrid misses as with Claude.
- **Outcome:** decided by the user: LLM search (Gemini, free tier) with the offline keyword -> fuzzy fallback; no embeddings in the app; no RAG.
- **What changed because of it:** entries 64, 71 and 75 were built and scored on this choice. Still open: whether the app should ask the LLM first or only after the local rules (entry 75: the hybrid's misses are all wrong local answers).
- **Links:** entries 57 to 59, 64, 71, 75; `phase2/request-matching/README.md`, `phase2/new/upload/README.md`, `.env.example`.

## 85. 25 to 26 Nov: other decisions by the user: scope, deliverables, which phones to carry, hosting

- **What we tried:** record the decisions that shaped the work of 25 and 26 Nov and that are not written in any other entry.
- **Why:** later readers (and the report) need to know who decided what, and why some promised parts are missing.
- **How / Result (decisions, in order):**
  - **Data notes (25 Nov):** cut every take into three 20 s pieces, drop the 23-Nov stairs and sr8/sr10/sr11 stops, remove obvious outliers: done as the `split20` variant (entry 40), with "do s8 10 11" read as dropping those study rooms (still to confirm, open question).
  - **No production app; at most 5 background agents (25 Nov).** App work only when the user asks for it explicitly (entry 83).
  - **Per-phone sheets are appended, never overwritten,** so progress over time can be shown in the presentation (dated tabs plus a Progress tab, entry 45). **Plain Excel, not Google Sheets;** a copy goes to the laptop's Downloads each time (entries 45, 51, 69, 72, 73).
  - **Which phones to carry on the night run (25 Nov):** both iPhones (only the iPhone barometer is reliable for floors, and a second same-handset session was the missing test); Android optional, and if any, the A56 (best Android then, two sessions); the A55 and S24 not worth carrying (weakest phones, S24 pressure unreliable, entry 30). The night run used ip18, ip16 and the A56 (entry 72).
  - **Push everything to GitHub (25 Nov).** Main was pushed as `c53481a` after a check for keys, chat text and very large files; from then on every landing was pushed. The `prep/*` and `feat/user-test` branches stay local.
  - **Hosting (25 Nov, question only, nothing built):** the user asked whether a server could replace the laptop hotspot, since phone and laptop lose each other across floors. Options listed: carry the laptop, a private network between phone and laptop (e.g. Tailscale), a public tunnel or a cloud server. Upload mode works with any of them; live mode needs the laptop to reach phyphox on the phone, so only the first two work for it. For the demo: carry the laptop.
  - **Labels and names (26 Nov):** the collector's own file names are ground truth (entry 69); delete duplicates only after a byte check (entry 68); disabled-toilet display names (entry 69).
  - **Explainer (26 Nov):** the user asked how training works (no GPU, no neural network: kNN on CPU) and for a visual explanation; a private explainer page was built outside the repo from the committed result files. It is not a repo file and adds no new numbers.
  - **A tidy-up of the user's whole personal folder** was requested and then put on hold by the user; it changed nothing in this repo (its `.gitignore` already covered `.env` since entry 71).
- **Outcome:** recorded. None of these decisions changed a number.
- **Links:** entries 40, 45, 51, 63, 68, 69, 71, 72, 83.

## 86. 25 Nov: live mode connected to a real phone for the first time (phyphox remote access); no scored live walk yet

- **Why:** live mode had only been tested on recorded data.

The app ran in live mode on the laptop. No walk with the truth noted was recorded, so there is no live accuracy number (`phase2/new/upload/live_runs/` does not exist).

- **What changed because of it:** use the address without `:8080` for phyphox remote access on this phone. The app server runs inside WSL on the laptop, so a phone cannot open the app page directly without extra network set-up; live mode works because the laptop reads the phone, not the other way round.

## 87. 26 Nov: team chat "Chris forgot the barometer, should the A56 redo everything?": what the files say

- **What we tried:** check the team-chat message of 26 Nov morning (Dicta: Chris forgot to turn on the barometer; should the A56 redo everything and the ip18 redo floor 5?) against the raw recordings, before anyone re-collects.
- **Why:** a redo costs a collection session; the night run's floor problem (entry 72) and the A56 problem might be mixed up.
- **How:** a one-off scratch script (not in the repo, run with `python -I`) listed the files inside every top-level raw phyphox ZIP, read the model from `meta/device.csv`, and counted ZIPs that contain a pressure sensor file, in `2026-11-24/sesi1/raw`, `2026-11-25/raw` (top level only, not inside Dicta's container ZIPs) and `2026-11-25-night/raw`.
- **Result:** A56 (SM-A566B): 0 of 55 (24 s1), 0 of 54 (25 Nov) and 0 of 58 (25 night) ZIPs contain pressure: the A56 has never logged a barometer in any session, so the night run is not different for it, and entries 28 and 74 already treat it as a phone without pressure. iPhone19,2 (Dicta): 55 of 55 (24 s1), 54 of 54 (25 Nov), but only 32 of 59 on the night run (27 without, the same count as `build_summary.json`); iPhone17,4 (Tian) 52 of 52 on the night run. The floor failure of the night (upload app 20/32, entry 72) comes from the missing pressure on Dicta's iPhone, not from the A56. The A56's real night problem is the magnetometer offset (median |B| 110.9 uT, `field_check.csv`, entry 72).

- **What changed because of it:** nothing in the data. Added to the open questions: whether the A56 can log pressure at all in the phyphox experiment the team uses, and that Dicta checks her phyphox set-up before the next collection.
- **Links:** `phase2/clean-data/2026-11-25-night/build_summary.json`, `field_check.csv`, `README.md`; `phase2/clean-data/2026-11-25/README.md` ("Only one floor sensor this session"); entries 28, 72, 73, 74.

## 89. 26 Nov: do ip16 (iPhone17,4) and ip18 (iPhone19,2) data pool? Tested on ip18 only: ip16 alone is clearly worse, pooling is a tie on top-1 (user question for the demo phone)

- **What we tried:** the user asked whether ip18 and ip16 are similar in accuracy and precision (if yes, train on both; if not, on ip18 only), because the demo will use Dicta's ip18. Two leave-one-session-out tests on the three ip18 sessions (`sesi1`, `25nov`, `25night`, 85 stops): training on (a) the other ip18 sessions only, (b) those plus all ip16 sessions, (c) all ip16 sessions only.
- **Why:** `out/eval.json` (entry 67) already said an ip16-trained model is worse on ip18 (57/85 vs 65/85), but nobody had tested the missing case, ip18 plus ip16 data together.
- **How:** (1) `phase2/new/upload/eval_iphone_pool.py` imports `walksense.py` read-only (app code unchanged) and uses the app recipe: 2 s windows, per-floor kNN, visit answer = mean kNN probability over its windows among the places on the true floor, xyz plus tilt and magnitude as in `eval.json`. Writes `phase2/new/upload/out/iphone_pool.json` (about 20 s). ip16 sessions = `sesi2`, `25night_tian`, `26tian` (74 stops); 23-Nov is NOT used because `walksense.ALL_SESSIONS` has no 23-Nov entry (its tree has `sensor_samples.csv` and no `clean/manifest.csv`). Macro precision / recall are over the (floor, place) classes with at least one true visit; a never-guessed class counts 0 precision. Exploratory: subtract each session x phone median xyz (test session included, so it is transductive). (2) `phase2/new/day3/eval_iphone_pool_fused.py` repeats it in the day-3 learned-weight fused kNN (imports `fused_knn.tune` and `outer_score` read-only; weights and k tuned by inner leave-one-session-out on the training pool; ip16 pool also has 23nov and 25spots); writes `phase2/new/day3/out-iphone-pool/` only, `out/` untouched (about 4.5 min). The clean sensor folders were copied from the main tree into the worktree, not rebuilt.
- **Result (app model, xyz, top-1 / top-3, `out/iphone_pool.json`; the (a) and (c) rows reproduce `eval.json` for all three kinds and all three held-out sessions, `check_all_match` true):**

| train set | top-1 | top-3 | macro precision | macro recall | per session top-1 (sesi1, 25nov, 25night) |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| (a) ip18 only | 65/85 | 73/85 | 0.739 | 0.750 | 21/27, 20/26, 24/32 |
| (b) ip18 + ip16 | 67/85 | 71/85 | 0.790 | 0.782 | 21/27, 21/26, 25/32 |
| (c) ip16 only | 57/85 | 70/85 | 0.637 | 0.670 | 19/27, 18/26, 20/32 |

  - (b) vs (a), per stop: 5 stops gained (4F disabled toilet, escalator down, Exit 10 door, LT2B, Y4502) and 3 lost (4F LT1B, 5F male disabled toilet, 5F Y5202); one stop is 1.2 points. (c) vs (a): 8 gained, 16 lost.
  - tilt top-1: (a) 41/85, (b) 41/85, (c) 32/85. Magnitude: 24/85, 24/85, 18/85.
  - Mirror test (supplementary, held-out ip16 sessions, 74 stops; a = ip16 only, b = pooled, c = ip18 only), xyz: 39/74, 42/74, 40/74; tilt 18/74, 25/74, 28/74.
  - Exploratory median-xyz offset, test on ip18: (a) 64/85, (b) 55/85, (c) 40/85 (pooling got worse by 9, not better); mirror 41/74, 41/74, 44/74. Not used for any recommendation.
  - Day-3 fused kNN (learned weights), test on the same 85 ip18 stops (`out-iphone-pool/iphone_pool_fused.json`): (A) ip18 only 59/85, top-3 66/85, macro precision 0.704, recall 0.702; (B) ip18 + ip16 58/85, top-3 75/85, 0.713 / 0.689; (C) ip16 only 44/85, top-3 63/85, 0.502 / 0.542; (D) all phones 56/85, top-3 77/85, 0.688 / 0.667. B vs A per stop: 8 gained, 9 lost. With the xyz group alone: A 56/85, B 53/85, C 42/85. Control: D should equal the committed `out/fused_sample_predictions.csv` (ip18, leave_one_session_out) at 57/85 top-3 75/85; the rerun gave 56/85 and 77/85, a 1-stop top-1 difference of the kind entry 77 calls the rerun noise floor.
- **Outcome:** success. ip16 and ip18 are not interchangeable: trained on ip16 alone, ip18 stops lose 8 stops in the app model (57 vs 65) and 15 in the fused kNN (44 vs 59), and macro precision drops about 0.10 and 0.20. Pooling ip16 into ip18 training is a tie on top-1 (app +2, fused -1; the 5 vs 3 and 8 vs 9 gains and losses are inside the noise), top-3 moves in opposite directions in the two models (app -2, fused +9), and the offset variant gets worse. Small n: 85 stops, 3 sessions.
- **What changed because of it:** recommendation for the demo: train on ip18 only (the demo phone), since the user's rule was "similar => both, otherwise ip18 only" and ip16 alone is clearly worse and pooling shows no reliable top-1 gain. The app is NOT changed (`pick_model` already uses the ip18-only model for an iPhone19,2 recording; to pool, `build_models` would need an ip18 model built from `training_stops(device=...)` of both iPhones, see the upload README).
- **Links:** `phase2/new/upload/eval_iphone_pool.py`, `phase2/new/upload/out/iphone_pool.json`, `phase2/new/day3/eval_iphone_pool_fused.py`, `phase2/new/day3/out-iphone-pool/`, `phase2/new/upload/README.md` (section "ip16 and ip18: pooled training?"), `out/eval.json`; commit on branch `feat/iphone-pool` (hash in the commit).

- **What we tried:** four user decisions of 27 Nov in the upload app, plus six README errors found by the runbook worker (entry 92): (1) wire the pre-registered floor rule (d) of entry 74 into the app's floor tracking, behind a switch with R0 still selectable, and re-check it with the app's own features; (2) the a56 model must not train on Chris's a56 night takes (entry 77, option B); (3) in Standard mode prefer ordinary toilets and use a disabled toilet only when no ordinary one fits; (4) README: port 80, `--device iPhone19,2`, real-phone status, WSL networking, the build step on a fresh clone, the `/config` hint.

(2) the a56 night magnetometer has a drifting hard-iron offset (entry 77; `phase2/clean-data/2026-11-25-night/field_check.csv`); the team headline already leaves those takes out. (3) "disabled toilets stay free for people who need them" (user). (4) the demo runbook could not be followed literally from the README.
- **How:**
  - **Floor rule** (`phase2/new/upload/walksense.py`): `FLOOR_RULE` (env `WALKSENSE_FLOOR_RULE`, `d` default, `R0` selectable); frozen parameters `VOTE_K, VOTE_P, VOTE_H, VOTE_M` = 15, 0.7, 2, 3 and `LONG_GAP_S` 15 min, copied from `floor/out/params.json`, nothing retuned. `Session.track_floor` (R0) is unchanged; new `Session.update_floor` = `pressure_step` (R0, and under (d) decides whether the stop asks the vote: no pressure, or one of the first 3 stops with pressure after a gap > 15 min since the last pressure reading; a pressure floor change ends that) + `apply_vote` (a vote counts at p >= 0.7 for the other floor; 2 in a row switch the floor and restart the pressure reference from this recording; a confident vote for the current floor resets the count). This follows `floor/rules.py` `comb` step for step. The vote is a new `FloorVote` (median imputer + scaler + kNN k 15, distance weights, label = floor) on the app's own 2 s xyz windows (first/last 5 s trimmed), built inside `PlaceModel` from the same training stops (same phone model; all phones if the model is unknown). `analyse()` (uploads) and `live.LiveTracker` (live: pressure at the first look at a stop as before, the vote once the stop has lasted `VOTE_AFTER_S` = 10 s) use it. `floor_rule_for(device)`: (d) for phones with a barometer; R0 for a55, a56 and the S24 unless `WALKSENSE_FLOOR_VOTE_NO_BARO=1` (see Result: the no-barometer half of the pre-registered decision rule does not pass); under that flag the S24 barometer is ignored as in (d).

It also checks that `rules.comb` with the app votes gives the same floors as the app's `Session` in `walksense.py evaluate` (`app_matches_eval_json`). Output `phase2/new/upload/out/floor_rule_check.json`.

## 91. 27 Nov: final headline configuration: Chris's a56 night takes excluded (user decision), the 178 vs 179 rerun difference found and fixed, official reruns, sheets and results pack

- **What we tried:** (1) make the user's 27-Nov decision the default: the official headline leaves out Chris's a56 (SM-A566B) night takes (entry 77 option B); (2) first answer entry 77's open question, why a rerun on identical inputs gave 179/411 against the committed 178/411, and make the pipeline deterministic; (3) rerun `run_all.sh` default and `--split20` on the final data and report the new headline; (4) append the sheets, rebuild the playgrounds and the results pack; (5) fold in entry 90 (the upload app's new `eval.json` layout) when main moved.
- **Why:** the headline numbers go into the report and slides, so they must use the agreed data and must come out the same on every rerun. Entry 77 had left a 1-take difference that nobody could explain.
- **How:**
  - Branch `feat/headline-final` in worktree `/home/basarah/personal/ge2340-final` (from main `38082d0`); the gitignored clean sensor folders (25-Nov night round/tian, 25-Nov spots, 26-Nov Tian) and `day3/cache/`, `cross-day/cache/` were copied from the main tree, not rebuilt. Python = the repo `.venv`.
  - **Exclusion.** New `phase2/new/day3/exclude_a56_night.csv`: every a56 recording of session `25night` (32 stops + 26 walks, from `clean-data/2026-11-25-night/round/clean/manifest.csv`; no a56 ZIP name is shared with an ip18 ZIP), each row with status `excluded_a56_hard_iron_offset` and the reason. `config.json` session `25night` gets `exclude_sources` (the mechanism of option B) and a `_comment_exclude` giving the reason (`field_check.csv`: a56 median |B| 110.9 uT that night against 40.6 and 45.2 uT on 24s1 and 25nov) and the entries. The raw ZIPs and clean files stay on disk and in the repo; the night WiGLE log is not excluded (separate file; the fused kNN uses only the S24 scanner). `a56_offset.py` now starts every option from `out-a56/config_A.json` (= config.json without this exclusion = the 26-Nov config), so A still means "keep" and the comparison reruns; B is checked against the official `out/`.
  - **Cause of 178 vs 179** (`day3/out-determinism/before_fix.json`). On main `38082d0` code and data, one BLAS thread, two runs: `fused_knn.main()` alone in a fresh process reproduced the committed files exactly (178/411, `fused_knn.json` and `fused_sample_predictions.csv` equal, 23/23 folds same weights and k); `evaluate.main()` then `fused_knn.main()` in ONE process (what `a56_offset.py run A` did) gave 179/411, 18/23 folds, the 25night fold k 3 and 23/64 instead of k 9 and 22/64, exactly entry 77's numbers. Cause: `gknn.py` drew the 3000 random pairs for each group's distance scale (median pair distance) from one module-level generator `np.random.default_rng(21)`; every `Bank.dists()` call advanced it, so a fold's scale depended on how many `dists()` calls the process had made before. `run_all.sh` runs each script in its own process, so the committed run itself was reproducible; threads were not the cause (entry 77's multi-thread A run matched its one-thread run).
  - **Fix.** `gknn.py`: every `dists()` call starts its own generator from `MEDIAN_SEED` 21 (same rows -> same pairs); `knn_scores()` breaks distance ties by training-row index (rows whose k-th distance is shared with an unselected row are re-selected with a stable full sort) and orders the neighbours with `lexsort`. `fused_knn.best()`: after sample accuracy, window accuracy and k nearest 5, ties go to the smaller k, then the lexicographically smallest weight vector (no dependence on dict order). `run_all.sh` exports one OMP/OpenBLAS/MKL thread unless the caller sets them. Commit `0846acf`.
  - **Official rerun.** Dated copies first: `out*/fused_sample_predictions_2026-11-27a.csv`, `out*/knn_playground_2026-11-27a.html` (the committed 26-Nov outputs). `PYTHON=.venv/bin/python sh phase2/new/day3/run_all.sh` and `... --split20` (exit 0 both; split20 also reran `upload_eval_split20.py`). Then `playground_check.py` in both variants; `perf_sheet.py --run-dir out --tab "2026-11-27 final default"` and `--run-dir out-split20 --tab "2026-11-27 final split20"` (data `23nov 24s1 24s2 25nov 25spots 25night(no a56) 25night_tian 26tian`; the requested tab name "2026-11-27 final, a56 night excluded" is longer than Excel's 31 characters, so the exclusion is in the Progress note); `gaps_sheet.py --perf-tab "2026-11-27 gaps final"`; `hard_places.py --label final_2026-11-27 out out-split20`.
  - **Determinism proof** (`day3/out-determinism/after_fix.json`, fixed code, final config): (a) `a56_offset.py run B` (evaluate then fused kNN in one process, derived config) vs official `out/`; (b) `fused_knn.py` alone with default multi-threading and `PYTHONHASHSEED=7`; (c) `a56_offset.py run B --split20` vs official `out-split20/`.
  - **a56 options rerun with the fixed code**: `a56_offset.py prepare`, `run A|B|C1 default|--split20`, `report`. C, C2 and C21 were not rerun: their `results.json` (gitignored) was in the entry-77 worktree, which no longer exists, so `report` now skips an option without `results.json`; their 26-Nov numbers are kept in `out-a56/comparison_2026-11-26.csv/.json`. Each run now writes `run_info.json` (branch HEAD when it finished). Fixed in `report`: `reproduces_official` counted the 0.0 inner-accuracy difference as a failure; only the true/false checks count now.
  - **Results pack** (`results-pack/build.py`, `config.json`, `timeline_facts.json`): numbers.md section 0 "Headline configuration" (the exclusion, read from day3 `config.json` `_comment_exclude` and the exclusion CSV, plus a note that the upload-app numbers are read from `upload/out/eval.json` at build time and that the app has its own session list, so this exclusion does not apply to it); per-phone accuracy with macro precision / recall (`derived.phone.*`, same definition as `perf_sheet.py`); section 16 and `table_a56_options.csv` + `fig_a56_options` for A / B / C1 from `out-a56/comparison.csv`; a timeline line for the decision and a 27-Nov milestone.
  - **Entry 90 landed during this job** (main `8803420`, merged as `469cf58`; entry 93 merged as `ed41f89`). `upload/out/eval.json` `floor_tracking` now holds the app default (rule d for barometer phones, 25night ip18 30/32) and `floor_tracking_by_rule` every phone under R0 and d. `build.py` now labels `up.floor.*` with the rule and its text from `floor_rule.rules` (it used to say "median-of-last-6" for every number), adds `up.floor_R0.*` / `up.floor_d.*` per phone and `up.train_exclude.*`, and the known-issues line says which rule is which. The renamed a56 keys (`a56 25nov->sesi1`, `a56 sesi1->25nov`) are read without change (the reader loops over every key). The 25-Nov timeline line about the upload app quoted `up.iphone sesi1+25nov->sesi2`, a key that the current eval no longer has; it showed "[n/a]" without being reported. It now reads the dated `eval_2026-11-26.json` (facts `up26.*`, config `upload_history_evals`), the 24/25-Nov floor line reads R0, and a missing timeline fact is now reported as missing. `floor/baseline.py` is in the other worker's folder and was not touched (open question below).
- **Result:**
  - **Leave one session out, learned-weight fused kNN** (`out/fused_knn.json`, `out-split20/fused_knn.json`; chance 1/26 = 3.8%): default **191/379 = 50.4%** (95% Wilson 45.4 to 55.4%), window accuracy 48.1%; split20 **527/1092 = 48.3%** (45.3 to 51.2%). Before (committed 26-Nov outputs): 178/411 = 43.3% and 553/1183 = 46.7%.
  - **Leaky vs honest** (default / split20): random windows (LEAKY) 1762/1778 = 99.1% / 3182/3227 = 98.6%; by recording (partly leaky) 190/379 = 50.1% / 601/1092 = 55.0%; grouped by stop 172/379 = 45.4% / 464/1092 = 42.5%; leave one session out 191/379 = 50.4% / 527/1092 = 48.3%.

## 92. 27 Nov: demo runbook for Dicta's iPhone 18 and a live-walk protocol with the scoring rule written before any walk

- **What we tried:** write down, as internal team notes, how to run the live demo on Dicta's iPhone 18 (night-before checklist, start commands, self-test, failure table, route, retakes) and how to run and score one live walk honestly. Docs only: no code was changed.
- **Why:** the demo uses one phone only (entry 89) and no scored live walk exists (entry 86). The live connection had worked once, with a port that the README still gets wrong, and a fresh clone of main does not start (entry 81). Writing the scoring rule first keeps the first live number honest.
- **How:**
  - Read the upload README, `server.py` (arguments), `live.py` (`probe`, state fields), `check_gemini.py`, entries 78, 79, 81, 86, 89 and `day3/out/fused_sample_predictions.csv` (scheme `leave_one_session_out`, phone `ip18`, 85 stops) for the per-place counts.
  - Ran what could be run without a phone, in a new worktree: `server.py --help`, `check_gemini.py` (in the main tree, which has the `.env`; prints only the key length), `live.py probe` against a closed port (fails with the message the runbook quotes), `walksense.build_models()` on a fresh worktree (reproduced `KeyError: 'mag'`, 11 s), then `build_night.py` and `build_tian26.py`, then `build_models()` again (43 s, trains), `powershell.exe -NoProfile -Command "arp -a"` (works from WSL), the planner offline through `search.search` / `search.plan_route`, and the arrival-marker and log shell loops against a closed port. No server was started on port 8000.
- **Result:**
  - Files: `phase2/new/DEMO-RUNBOOK.md`, `phase2/new/live-walk/PROTOCOL.md`, `phase2/new/live-walk/score_sheet.csv` (header only).
  - Weak iPhone 18 places in the day-3 fused kNN leave-one-session-out file, recomputed on 27 Nov: escalator_down_4F 0/3, escalator_down 0/2, lt1a 1/3, sr9 2/4, y5202 2/4, disabled_toilet 2/4, lift_stairs 2/4, y5401 2/4 (matches the numbers the user gave). Places with all stops right: canteen 4/4, exit_5 3/3, male_toilet 3/3, escalator_up 2/2, lt2a 3/3, escalator_up_4F 3/3, entrance_exit 3/3. These counts are from the fused kNN, not from the app's own model (entry 89: 65/85 vs 59/85 top-1), and have 2 to 4 stops per place.
  - Offline planner checks used in the self-test: Canteen -> LT1a Standard 36.6 m / 48.2 s (escalator), Step-free 65.0 m / 92 s (lift); Female toilet from the Canteen: Female toilet (5F); Step-free + Female: Mixed disabled toilet (4F), about 30 m / 66 s.
  - Suggested demo route and protocol route (7 stops): entrance_exit, lt2a, escalator_up_4F, ride up, escalator_up, canteen, exit_5, male_toilet. Scoring: lit place at 10 s after arrival equals the true place; not lit counts as wrong; 3 scored walks planned; all reported; pass bar to be written before walk 1.
  - **README (`phase2/new/upload/README.md`) is wrong or out of date in these places** (not edited, docs-only job): (1) the phyphox address in "Live mode" steps 2 to 4 and the probe/server commands use port `:8080`, but this phone served on port 80 (entry 86); (2) `--device` defaults to `iPhone17,4` and the README start command omits `--device iPhone19,2`; (3) "Not confirmed against a real phone yet" is out of date: the probe and live mode ran on a real phone on 25 Nov (entry 86), only a scored walk and the pocket question are missing; (4) "Open the printed address ... on the phone" does not hold when the server runs in WSL: the printed address is WSL's internal one (entry 86); (5) "Run it" does not say that a fresh clone needs `build_night.py` and `build_tian26.py` first (entry 81); (6) the `/config` hint says `:8080/config`.
- **Outcome:** success for the docs. Not tested: a real phone session on 27 Nov, the phone opening the page through Windows port forwarding or WSL mirrored networking, the pocket question, and the 10 s snapshot rule on real live data. The scoring script mentioned in the protocol does not exist yet.
- **What changed because of it:** nothing in code. After entries 90/91 land (floor rule (d) in the app, a56 night excluded from the app's a56 model, Standard prefers ordinary toilets, final day-3 headline without the a56 night) the runbook says to pull, rerun the self-test and recheck the toilet answers and the per-place counts.
- **Links:** `phase2/new/DEMO-RUNBOOK.md`, `phase2/new/live-walk/PROTOCOL.md`, `phase2/new/live-walk/score_sheet.csv`; entries 26, 71, 78, 79, 81, 84, 86, 89; branch `docs/demo-runbook`.

## 93. 27 Nov: upload app redesigned map-first (like Google Maps); search reads the start (From) as well as the destination (To) and shows no LLM text; From = "Current location"; "Step-free" renamed "Disabled"; nearest exit; Nearest-toilet button, Any WC and hint lines removed

- **What we tried:** the user's nine requests of 27 Nov (their own words, summarised): (1) do not show the LLM's reply text, the search should just set From / To and draw the route; (2) make the page like Google Maps: full-width map, search on top, a compact route card (from -> to, distance, time, floor change), phone (390 px) first, laptop fine, no new features; (3) the LLM search must find FROM as well as TO ("im in y5202, find me the nearest exit" -> From = Y5-202, To = the nearest exit), offline too; (4) keep the From / To lists, add "Current location" to From as the default (the place the live model thinks the phone is at), say clearly when there is no fix and draw no route then; (5) rename "Step-free" to "Disabled" everywhere the user sees it, behaviour unchanged; (6) the "Nearest toilet" text that cannot be clicked; (7) delete "Any WC", keep a simple "WC: Male | Female" toggle; (8) remove the hint lines at the bottom ("Tap a place to set To / From" and the ones below it); (9) keep the phyphox live status but in a dropdown closed by default.
- **Why:** the page had grown one feature at a time (entries 70, 71, 78, 90) into a long form with the map in the middle, chat-like answer text and help lines; the demo (entry 92) runs on a phone-sized screen.
- **How:**
  - **Search (`phase2/new/upload/search.py`, `phase2/request-matching/match.py`).** App prompt v2 (`ASK_PROMPT_VERSION = 'v2'`): Gemini answers JSON `{"from_id", "place_id"}` (ID or NONE each) and no sentence any more (v1 asked for one sentence the page showed); `from_id` only when the query says where you are / start, never guessed; for "the nearest X" it may pick any X because the app picks the nearest by route. `parse_ask_route` (place_id required, from_id optional, a v1 `answer` still accepted; `parse_ask` kept for the v1 interface). The in-memory cache key is now (prompt version, normalised query) and stores (from, to, answer); errors are still never cached. Offline: `split_query` reads the start from "from X to Y", "Y from X", "I'm in/at/near X", "starting at X", "dari X ke Y", "aku/saya di X"; the start text must resolve by the whole-word keyword match (not fuzzy, so "I'm in a hurry" does not become "hungry" = canteen); if it does not resolve ("I'm on 4F", "from here", "from the lower floor"), From stays and the whole query is matched exactly as before. The query's start wins over the From list on the first run; the page reruns with `qfrom=0` after the user changes From (then the list wins). `search()` returns `from_query`, `to` (`{id,name,floor}` or `{nearest: toilet|exit}`), `waiting`, `exit`.
  - **Nearest exit (`phase2/new/routing/route.py`).** New `EXITS` table (the 5 exit nodes of graph.json; `emergency` = tagged emergency in `destinations.csv`: Exit 5 on 5F and 4F) and `nearest_exit(start, mode, emergency, floor)`, ranked by route cost like `nearest_toilet`. In `search.py` a request for an exit in general (exit / exits / way out / get out / keluar / 出口 / emergency words, no named exit) goes to the nearest exit from the start; "emergency" / "fire" / "evacuate" words -> emergency exits only; "Exit 5", "Exit 10", "gate", "entrance", "main" keep the named exit. Decision taken here (no user rule existed): a plain "nearest exit" uses all 5 exits, because only Exit 5 is tagged emergency in our data; the user's example said "nearest emergency exit", so both answers are reported below.
  - **Current location (`search.py`, `server.py`).** `from=here` = the tracked place, or no start at all (`source: 'waiting'`, no route, `waiting: true`) on `/api/route`, `/api/nearest` and `/api/search`. API calls without `from` keep the old default (lit place, else the 4F gate), so older scripts and tests are unchanged. `server.tracked_place` now remembers the last place the live model lit (`LIVE['fix']`), so a route from Current location does not vanish at the first step; `/api/live` and `/api/state` return `fix`; New session clears it.
  - **Page (`phase2/new/upload/index.html`, rewritten layout; map drawing, upload, live polling and route drawing code kept).** Top bar: search + Go, From (default "Current location (<place>)" or "(waiting)"), To (any place, plus "Nearest toilet"), Standard | Disabled, "WC: Male | Female". Map fills the width (54svh on a phone, full height on a laptop >= 900 px with a 400 px left panel); zoom top left; 5F / 4F / Pic top right with an orange ring on the other floor's button when the route continues there; a chip bottom right with the floor and the lit place (%). Route card below the map: "From → To", metres · time, floor change or "5F, no floor change", a "Disabled: lifts only" tag, and a closed "Steps ▸" with the step list, connector costs, toilet policy note, other options and which search method answered. "Waiting for location" in the card when Current location has no fix; the route then appears when a place lights up (live poll or upload). "Live sensor data ▸" is a `<details>` closed by default. Upload, stop/walk result, floor fix, New session and the tested-accuracy list follow below the card.
  - **Item 6, decided by the user during the job:** the clickable "Nearest toilet" button in the chip row worked when tested (headless click on port 8041 drew the route to the Female toilet, 46 m from the default gate); the text that could not be clicked was the grey "Nearest toilet" method badge on the result card, styled like a chip. The worker first planned to remove only the badge; the advisor then passed on the **user's decision: remove the "Nearest toilet" button/text completely; toilet routing is automatic-nearest** whenever To is a toilet in general (search "toilet" / "wc" / "bathroom", or To = "Nearest toilet") under the WC toggle and the mode (Standard: ordinary first, entry 90; Disabled: disabled toilets only); a specific toilet only when one is named or picked ("the 4F toilet", "male toilet", To = Female toilet (5F)). Both the button and the badge are gone; To got a "Nearest toilet" option.
  - **Item 7:** "WC: Male | Female"; tapping the pressed one again = neither = any toilet that fits the mode, i.e. what "Any WC" did, and still the default (stored as `any`, so old `localStorage` values still work).
  - **Item 5:** user-visible "Step-free" -> "Disabled": the chip, the card tag, `POLICY_NOTE` ("choose Disabled mode ..."), the excluded reason ("no route in Disabled mode"), the escalator answer note, `planner_samples.json` `toilet_policy`. **Internal names stay** (`accessible` profile, `mode=accessible` in the API, `localStorage` value), so no API or test changed meaning.
  - **Items 8 and 1:** removed the "Tap a place to set To / From" switch (tapping a place now always sets To; From is set with the list or in the query), the map note under the map ("Send a stop to see where you are.", "Route ... continues on 4F (tap the 4F map button)"; status now in the chip, floor continuation as the ring), the legend line (solid / dashed / dotted corridors), the method badge and the answer sentence. Kept: "Gemini not used / off" moved into Steps ▸.
  - Worktree `/home/basarah/personal/ge2340-team/.claude/worktrees/agent-a2ae3625309eb75dd`, branch `feat/ui-gmaps` from main 8803420. The gitignored clean sensor folders (25-Nov night round + tian, 26-Nov Tian, 25-Nov spots) were copied from the main tree, not rebuilt. UI checks on ports 8041 (no live) and 8042 (a fake live tracker standing at Y5-202, scratch launcher outside the repo, place models not trained); the live demo server on port 8000 was not touched (test servers stopped by exact PID).
  - **Interruptions (save-the-story):** the job stopped once on a usage limit while writing the new page; the advisor resumed it after the limit reset. Nothing was lost (edits were on disk). The worktree guard refused several shell commands (heredocs and `cd` + git in one line), so file edits were made with small Python scripts kept in the scratchpad; one advisor note mentioned a stray `search_fn.py`: it was a scratch file in the session scratchpad, never in the repo tree.
- **Result:**
  - **Request-matching eval, rerun (prompt and matcher unchanged, `evaluate.py --method gemini|hybrid_gemini --no-llm-calls`, 0 API calls, all from `llm_cache_gemini_v1.json`):** gemini 124/128 (easy 23/23, hard 101/105, dev 64/66, test 60/62), hybrid_gemini 115/128 (22/23, 93/105, 59/66, 56/62): identical to entry 75. This eval scores `LLM_SYSTEM`, not the app's prompt, so it cannot change; the rerun only rewrote latency and call-count fields of `out/gemini_results.json`, so that file was restored to entry 75's version (its `api_calls_in_last_run` 28 / `sleep_s` 4.5 record the real run). **The app's v2 prompt was not scored on the 128 queries** (that would be about 128 live calls; the free tier and the "few live calls" rule): open below.
  - **The app's offline search on the 128 to-only queries (`phase2/new/upload/eval_search_app.py` -> `out/search_app_eval.json`, Gemini off, scored with `evaluate.is_ok`):** start = default 4F gate 96/128 -> 99/128 (10 answers changed), Canteen 95/128 -> 97/128 (2 changed), LT1a 96/128 -> 98/128 (10 changed). Every change is an exit query now answered with the nearest exit, plus q056 "the exti" and q087 "出口喺邊" that used to find nothing; no query was found to name a start (only q118 "escalator going up from the lower floor" splits, and "the lower floor" is not a place, so it is unchanged). From the gate, q012 "pintu keluar" (expects the gate) becomes right only because the gate is the nearest exit there; from LT1a it is wrong both before (Exit 5) and after (Exit 10 4F).
  - **From + To set (`from_to_queries.csv`, 24 queries drafted by the agent from the user's example and the rules above, expected values written before the first run):** start 24/24, destination 23/24. The miss is ft21 = test-set q118: the keyword matcher answers the 4F down escalator instead of the 4F up escalator, as before entry 93 (not caused by this change).
  - **User's example:** "im in y5202, find me the nearest exit" -> From Room Y5-202, To Exit 10 (by the toilets), 15.1 m / 11.6 s (via the female toilet). "... nearest emergency exit" -> Exit 5 (emergency exit), 37.8 m / 29.1 s. From LT1a: nearest exit = Exit 10 (4F) 5.9 m; emergency = Exit 5 (4F) 31.3 m (`routing/out/planner_samples.json` `exits`).
  - **Live Gemini, 2 calls (`check_gemini_fromto.py`, `out/search_samples_gemini_v2.json`; key read from the main tree's `.env` into the process only, never printed or copied):** "im in y5202, find me the nearest exit" from Current location without a fix: raw reply `{"from_id": "y5202", "place_id": "exit_10"}`, 2.427 s, model gemini-3.5-flash-lite; the app: method gemini, From Y5-202 (from the query), To Exit 10, 15.1 m / 11.6 s. To-only "where is lecture theatre 1a" with From = Canteen: raw `{"from_id": "NONE", "place_id": "lt1a"}`, 2.468 s; the app keeps From = Canteen, To LT1a, 36.6 m / 48.2 s by the down escalator.
  - **Tests:** `routing/test_route.py` 16 OK (was 14: exit table = graph exits + destinations tags; nearest exit for every start x mode x emergency x floor against an independent minimum); `upload/test_search.py` 24 OK (was 17: Current location waiting on all three endpoints, live fix and last fix while walking, chosen From wins, the page wording; start + destination offline (incl. Indonesian, "only a start", four non-place starts), to-only keeps From, nearest exit for every start x mode, nearest toilet with a start and WC filter, Gemini v2 replies mocked incl. a v1-style reply, a bad start id falling back offline and `qfrom=0`); `request-matching/test_gemini.py` 16 OK (was 15: v2 parser); `upload/test_walksense.py` + `test_live.py` 16 OK in 184.8 s (unchanged code paths).
  - **Screenshots** (`phase2/new/upload/screenshots/`, headless Chromium 1234 via playwright-core from the scratchpad, served by `server.Handler` without place models): `v6-phone-390-from-query-nearest-exit.png` (+ `-dark`), `v6-phone-390-waiting-for-location-dark.png`, `v6-phone-390-current-location-disabled-to-lt1a.png` (fake live fix at Y5-202, Disabled, lift, 92 m), `v6-phone-390-toilet-female-steps-open.png`, `v6-phone-390-live-sensor-data-open.png`, `v6-laptop-1280-current-location-toilet.png`. Page width = viewport width on every shot (no horizontal scroll); no console errors after a blank favicon link was added (the first run logged one 404 for `/favicon.ico`, as in entry 90).
- **Outcome:** success for the page, the from/to search and the nearest exit, offline and in a two-query live check. Not done: scoring the app's v2 prompt on the 128 queries with Gemini, and a look on a real phone browser (only headless 390 px).
- **What changed because of it:** the demo page is map-first; search acts instead of answering; From defaults to Current location; the Disabled name; toilets and exits go to the nearest one by route unless one is named. Docs: `upload/README.md` (new section "The page", search, route planner, API), `routing/README.md` (Disabled wording, Exits), `DEMO-RUNBOOK.md` self-test steps 2 to 9 and the failure table, `PROJECT-STORY.md` 3.15.

## 94. 27 Nov: demo runbook numbers moved to the final entry-91 run, floor `baseline.py` fixed, and (user decision) every exit is an emergency exit

- **What we tried:** three small jobs on branch `docs/runbook-final-numbers` (from main `a9326d3`, which has the entry-91 merge). (1) Make `DEMO-RUNBOOK.md` sections e and f quote the final official day-3 run instead of the 26-Nov file; entry 93's edits to the file stay. (2) Make `floor/baseline.py` read the R0 numbers from where entry 90 moved them. (3) Extra scope from the user during the job (27 Nov): all exits are emergency exits, so "nearest emergency exit" must pick the nearest of all exits by route, not only Exit 5.
- **Why:** entry 91 changed the default ip18 per-place numbers, so the runbook's weak-place list and retake list were out of date; `baseline.py` had been reporting a false 25night mismatch since entry 90; entry 93 had left open whether Exit 10 and the gate are emergency exits (only Exit 5 was tagged), and the user decided that they are.
- **How:**
  - **Runbook numbers.** Recomputed with pandas from `phase2/new/day3/out/fused_sample_predictions.csv` (scheme `leave_one_session_out`, phone `ip18`; 85 stops, sessions 24s1, 25night, 25nov; 26 places) and from `out-split20/fused_sample_predictions.csv`, never from the task text. Edited the header line, the "two changes land" paragraph (they have landed), the evidence paragraph (ip18 headline, what moved), the weak / next-weakest / stronger lists, the "avoid" line and the section f retake table and its "why" line. The route table and every self-test step of entry 93 are unchanged.
  - **`floor/baseline.py`.** R0 is now compared with `floor_tracking_by_rule[<name> iphone]['R0']['stops']`, falling back to `floor_tracking[<name> iphone]['stops_correct']` when the by-rule block is absent. Ran it before and after the edit. `floor/README.md`: the old sentence about the false mismatch is replaced and a short section on `DAY3_CONFIG` is added at the end (note only; the floor study was not rerun).
  - **Emergency exits (user decision 27 Nov).** The tag is data, so it was changed where the data lives: `request-matching/destinations.csv` gets `emergency` on `exit_10`, `exit_10_4F` and `entrance_exit` (`exit_5` and `exit_5_4F` already had it; these five are all the exit nodes of `graph.json`), and `request-matching/build_destinations.py` (which writes that file) the same; running it reproduces the hand-edited file byte for byte. `routing/route.py` `EXITS` has `emergency: True` for all five (its table is checked against the csv by `test_route.py`), comments updated in `route.py` and `upload/search.py`. The search code needed no logic change: `EMERGENCY_WORDS` still decide whether the emergency filter is asked for, and the filter now lets every exit through. The map's own label "Exit 5 (emergency exit)" in `graph.json` and on the page is left as it is.
  - **Did the tag change touch the matcher?** The offline matchers use the tag set as a tie-break (`match.keyword_match`, `keyword_v1_match`, `tokens_match`, `fuzzy_keyword_match`). Ran all four over the 128 `test_queries.csv` rows with the old and the new `destinations.csv`: 0 answers changed in each. The cached LLM answers (`llm_cache_*.json`) were made with the old tags in the prompt and are keyed by query only, so they were not redone; the published request-matching results were not rerun (embedding and LLM methods need model downloads or calls). A rerun with the new file could differ for the LLM rows.
  - **Tests and samples.** `routing/test_route.py`: the old assertion "emergency from Y5-202 = Exit 5" now asserts Exit 10, 15.1 m, nothing excluded, all 5 as options, and all exits tagged; the filter itself is still tested with a table that has no emergency exit. `upload/test_search.py` `test_nearest_exit`: "im in y5202, where is the nearest emergency exit" -> Exit 10, 15.1 m; from LT1a "nearest emergency exit" -> Exit 10 (4F); "fire exit" from the Canteen equals "nearest exit" from the Canteen. `python phase2/new/routing/route.py samples` rewrote `routing/out/planner_samples.json`. READMEs: `routing/README.md` (Exits paragraph) and `upload/README.md` (Nearest exit bullet).
- **Result:**
  - **ip18 per place, final default run** (`day3/out`, leave one session out, ip18 **60/85 = 70.6%**, top-3 73/85; the entry-91 headline). Weakest: escalator_down 0/2 (the only place at 0), escalator_down_4F 1/3, then 2/4: sr9, disabled_toilet, lift_stairs, y5401. 2 of 3: disabled_toilet_4F, exit_5_4F, lt2b, y4502, lt1b, exit_10_4F, y5302, female_toilet, lt1a. 3 of 4: exit_10, y5302_back, lift_stairs_4F, y5202. All stops right: canteen 4/4, exit_5 3/3, male_toilet 3/3, escalator_up 2/2, lt2a 3/3, escalator_up_4F 3/3, entrance_exit 3/3. **Before (runbook, 26-Nov file) -> after:** y5202 2/4 -> 3/4, escalator_down_4F 0/3 -> 1/3, lt1a 1/3 -> 2/3; every other place equals the runbook's old number. Split20 ip18 (175/253 = 69.2%): escalator_down 0/6 and escalator_down_4F 0/9 are the weakest, then disabled_toilet_4F 4/9; the runbook quotes this only as a cross-check.
  - **Runbook retake list (section f)** is now 6 places (2/4 or worse): escalator_down, escalator_down_4F, sr9, disabled_toilet, lift_stairs, y5401. lt1a (2/3) and y5202 (3/4) left the old list of 8; lt1a stays in the "avoid as live-lit" line. **The demo route is unchanged**: all 7 stops of the route (entrance_exit, lt2a, escalator_up_4F, escalator_up, canteen, exit_5, male_toilet) have the same ip18 counts as before and are still the places with every stop right.
  - **`baseline.py`:** before the edit 16 of 17 comparisons matched and `r0_upload 25night 20/32 MISMATCH`; after: `r0_upload` 24s1 27/27, 24s2 26/26, 25nov 26/26, 25night 20/32, 25night_tian 23/23, 26tian 25/25 all match, `r1_day3` 11 of 11 match, no MISMATCH line, exit status 0. `out/baseline.json` is byte-identical to the committed file, and also identical when run with `DAY3_CONFIG=out-a56/config_A.json` (17 of 17 either way). With that variable `floorlib.recordings()` has 58 a56 and 59 iPhone rows for 25night, without it 0 a56 and 59 iPhone, which is the reason for the README note.
  - **Emergency exits:** from Y5-202 the nearest emergency exit is now Exit 10 (by the toilets) 15.1 m / 11.6 s (entry 93: Exit 5 37.8 m / 29.1 s); from LT1a Exit 10 (4F) 5.9 m (was Exit 5 (4F) 31.3 m); from the 4F gate and from Exit 10 itself the exit you stand at, 0 m, same as the plain nearest exit (was Exit 5 (4F) 14.6 m and Exit 5 22.7 m). In `planner_samples.json` 10 of the 36 `exits` rows changed (all with emergency yes; starts y5202, exit_10, lt1a, entrance_exit, both modes; y5202 appears twice in the sample starts) and the emergency answer now equals the plain answer for every start and mode. Suites with the repo venv: `routing/test_route.py` 16 OK, `upload/test_search.py` 24 OK, `upload/test_walksense.py` + `test_live.py` 16 OK (114 s), `request-matching/test_gemini.py` 16 OK.
- **Outcome:** success. Failures and dead ends: (1) My first `baseline.py` rerun in the fresh worktree wrote a **wrong `out/baseline.json`** (thresholds 0.301 to 0.313 instead of 0.292 to 0.307 and the two `25spots` floor rows missing, while still reporting a match): the gitignored 25spots sensor folders `floor4` / `floor5` were not in the new worktree, the day-3 loader took the session as empty without stopping, and the cached feature table was built from that. I noticed because the diff showed changed thresholds; I copied the two folders from the main tree, deleted `day3/cache/` (gitignored) and reran: the file is then identical to the committed one. A fresh worktree needs those folders too (the runbook's rebuild commands cover only the night and Tian sessions); the silent empty load is not fixed here. (2) `test_walksense.py` first failed 3 of 10 with "cleaned sensor CSVs are not built in this tree" until `build_night.py` and `build_tian26.py` had been run, as the runbook says. (3) The task text suggested `DAY3_CONFIG=phase2/new/day3/out-a56/config_A.json`; `data.py` resolves a relative value against the `day3/` folder, so the working form is `DAY3_CONFIG=out-a56/config_A.json` (or an absolute path), and the README says so.
- **What changed because of it:** the runbook's weak places and retake list; the emergency-exit tag in the data and the app's answers; `baseline.py` runs clean. LT1a and Y5-202 are off the retake list. The circle-UI mock (`circle-ui/places_data.js`, built by `make_mock.py` from `destinations.csv` aliases) was not regenerated: it is not part of the app.
