## Lessons learned

- **Same-recording splits give fake accuracy.** 94% (pilot) became 78% on later revisits and 18 to 24% on another day, for the same kind of features. Always report the honest split and label the leaky one as leaky.
- **Check how a label was made before trusting it.** A rule that looked fine ("stop = origin of next walk") silently mislabelled stops with no walk after them. The collector's route list fixed 6 of 42 labels.
- **A fixed pressure threshold fails because of weather; compare consecutive stops instead.** Step tracking got 42/42 and 26/26.
- **A log that "covers" a stop is not a fingerprint.** WiGLE rows are not repeated scans. Count repeated scans, not rows.
- **Log everything at stops that you log on walks.** Not having the accelerometer at stops blocked the tilt correction.
- **Use one phone for the whole protocol.** Two phones disagreed (r = 0.20).
- **Small n.** 9 revisit visits and 17 cross-day visits: one visit is 6 to 11%. Present them as first estimates.
- **A tracker needs a way back.** Limiting moves to neighbouring circles without a small "could be anywhere" share meant one wrong stop was never corrected (entry 20, v1).
- **A map can't replace a fingerprint.** Where a place had no fingerprint in training, the graph did not find it (0/22 on 23 Nov); it helps most when every place has a fingerprint and the magnetometer is weak (cross-day, area 12/17 vs 7/17).
- **Merging places only renames errors inside an area.** graph-v2 gained one exact cross-day visit and no area visits (entry 29); a merge can't fix a weak fingerprint, and "revisits" created by merging different spots are not real revisits.
- **Group by the thing that is shared, not by the file.** Wi-Fi and the floor belong to a stop, not to a phone: holding out one phone of a 4-phone stop gave 55% instead of 29% (entry 32). Random window splits gave 99.7%.
- **Check the device ID, not the phone's nickname.** "iphone" was two handsets on 24 Nov; the same handset across days gave 13/26 and 18/42, a different one less (entry 30). Pooling all phones in one kNN was worse than one model per phone (entry 32).
- **Step tracking on the barometer breaks over long pauses.** Weather moved the pressure 0.833 hPa during a break and four stops went to the wrong floor (entry 30).
- **A same-day win can vanish across days.** Tilt correction helped on 23-Nov walk endpoints (39% vs 0%) but added nothing across days or at stops (entry 30).
- **Check the check.** The first sklearn comparison of the playground disagreed in 8 of 720 cases because the reference mishandled a missing feature (entry 35); compare the missing-data rules, not only the maths.
- **Put the filter rule in the code that is tested.** The playground's training-mask rule now lives in the checked block and the check compares it with an independent mask (entry 36); a mask bug would otherwise hide behind a correct kNN.
- **A device ID in the metadata beats a label in a README.** The phone is now read per recording from each ZIP's `device.csv` and checked against the manifests (entry 37); the numbers did not change because each session had one iPhone, but the interactive pages and the "same phone" tests had been mixing two handsets.
- **Most of the |B| spread is the handset and the day, not the place.** At the default settings the place explains 13.8% of |B| and 55.2% of x/y/z, while phone plus session explain 82.9% of |B| (entry 38); the per-place table shows which places drift most (the escalators, the disabled toilet).
- **Hold the phone the same way and raw x/y/z works.** With the phone facing the door at every stop, x/y/z got 17/26 and 17/27 across sessions (entry 24) where it got 18 to 24% across days without that rule.
- **Compare pressure with a few recent readings, not one.** A single odd reading near a vent flipped the floor; the median of the last 6 did not (entry 24).
- **Replay recorded data on its real clock.** Squeezing out the breaks made weather drift look like a floor change (entry 26).
- **A few seconds of standing hold almost all the place information.** 3 s gave the same top-1 as 10 s (entry 27); the field at a spot does not change.
- **More pieces of the same take are not more evidence.** Splitting 60 s takes into three 20 s samples tripled n but not the information; removing pieces raised accuracy mostly by removing hard test pieces (entry 40). Run a control and compare on the same test pieces before calling a cleaning step an improvement.
- **Number log entries only when they land on the shared branch.** Two people adding "entry 24" on different machines collided; the public numbers won and ours moved by +4 (entry 39), and again by +1 when Dicta's entry 28 landed (entry 48).
- **Name the hard cases before adding the data that should fix them.** The never-correct list was written down before the 25-Nov rerun (entry 41), so the after numbers (female_toilet 0/7 -> 5/9, lift_stairs still 0/9) cannot be cherry-picked (entry 44).
- **A missing value in one phone can break a median.** One S24 stop without pressure made the S24 floor threshold `nan` and moved two old floor scores; use nan-safe statistics and diff the old numbers after adding data (entry 44).
- **New data can help some places and hurt others.** Adding 25 Nov fixed female_toilet, but y5401 and the disabled toilet lost their correct predictions; check per place, not only the total (entry 44).
- **Compare phones on the same settings, in one place.** Pick the held-out rule once and show every ticked phone under it (entry 46); the training mode matters more than expected for phones with one session (same phone only: a55 has no leave-one-session-out test).
- **Hash a second export before adding it.** All 36 round recordings of our WhatsApp export were byte-identical to Dicta's set; adding both would have put the same take in training and test (entry 49).
- **Recompute the hashes before deleting a "duplicate".** The duplicate list was recomputed from the bytes of both sets (including her container ZIPs) before 36 files were removed; the match on name, device and START was not trusted (entry 68).
- **A build step that rebuilds a folder deletes what you add to it.** The duplicates list written into `clean/` was removed by the next `build_clean.py`; outputs of other scripts go next to the folder, not inside it (entry 49).
- **A complete pass beats more spot recordings of a few places.** Dicta's full 26-place pass ended the zero-correct places; the partial export had no takes at most of them (entries 44, 50).
- **Fix the thresholds before looking at the sheet.** The data-gaps rules (MISSING, LACKING, WEAK) were written down first, so the to-collect list cannot be tuned to a wanted answer (entry 51).
- **Write failures down while they are fresh.** They are the reason the day-3 checklist has the rules it has.
- **Check the heuristic on rounded data.** The first A* admissibility check failed by under 0.01 s because the scale used metres while edges store rounded seconds (entry 53); a test over every pair caught it.
- **An LLM given a correct corridor list does not invent corridors; without one it does.** Haiku: 0 bad hops of 203 with distances, 59 of 93 with only a place list (entry 55).
- **Re-run the committed results before building on them.** The starter request-matching `results.csv` said 23/23 while the committed code gave 18/23 (entry 56).
- **Substring alias rules hide bugs.** "eat" matched inside "theatre"; whole-word matching fixed it (entry 58). Typos and non-English words are where keyword rules fall (typo 4/18 hard queries, entry 59).
- **A cut-off tuned on a small dev split may not carry over.** The embedding "none" threshold gave 52/66 on dev and 44/62 on test (entry 59).
- **Smoothing fixes jitter, not bias.** A 1 s kNN averaged over the last ticks scored the same as no averaging (entry 61): the wrong answers at a stop were steady, not jumpy. The jumps were on walks, and the fix was to feed the model only when the phone is still. A constant per-phone offset made things worse.
- **Generate the numbers sheet from the files.** `phase2/new/results-pack/build.py` keeps every quoted number tied to a source file, a commit and a test definition (entry 62); quote chance and n together, because the chance level differs between files.
- **A cascade is only as good as its first confident answer.** The hybrid's 13 misses were all wrong local matches that never reached the LLM (entry 64).
- **Keep the old output and prove the old mode still reproduces it.** The app's 24-only mode matched the old `eval.json` key by key and `live_checks.json` byte for byte before the new numbers were quoted (entry 66).
- **Guessing a label from timing is not a label.** Three S24 labels inferred from nearby iPhone recordings were all wrong once the user's own file names were found (entry 69); a label needs a name from the person who stood there, and the label's source belongs in the file (`label_source`).
- **Change display names, not ids.** The two disabled toilets were renamed for display only (`config.json` `place_display`), so every key, comparison and old output keeps its meaning (entry 69).
- **A sketch drawn on a map screenshot is a survey in disguise.** The Google screenshot under the collector's drawing is to scale and north-up (0.75 m RMS against the Google pins), so it gave a metric frame for both floors and real room shapes (entry 70).
- **Put the key in one ignored file and make the app work without it.** Search falls back to keyword -> fuzzy on no key, no internet, a timeout or a bad reply, so the demo cannot break on the LLM (entry 71).
- **Check each phone's raw field before training on it.** Chris's a56 read a median |B| of 110.9 uT on the night run against 40 to 45 uT on the other days (`field_check.csv`), and its 32 night takes scored 2/32. A one-line check of median |B| per phone and session (`check_field.py`) would have caught it at ingest (entry 72).
- **A floor tracker that keeps the floor when pressure is missing fails quietly.** 13 of Dicta's 32 night stops had no barometer, including the 5F -> 4F change, so the upload app stayed on 5F for 12 stops (entry 72).
- **When the file names say nothing, match the label trail strictly.** Tian's phone saved 51 of 52 files as "point <time>"; his chat captions became labels only through a one-to-one, in-order match that fails loudly (gap 7 to 45 s, each caption before the next file), and each caption's stop/walk was checked against the duration (entry 73). Name the recording in phyphox before saving when possible.
- **A second pass on the same phone settles a doubtful label better than a guess.** Tian's morning LT stops put his doubtful night "lt2b" 3.0 uT from LT1B and far from LT2B (entry 73); the walk-endpoint version of the check failed its own control, so only the stops count as evidence.
- **Pre-register a fallback and test it where the main signal is complete too.** On the 25-night dropout the day-3 step tracker (+9 stops), the drift-extrapolated reference (+8) and the pressure-else-magnetic rule (+10) all beat the app, but the first two lose the 24-s1 weather stops that the app already handled; only the rule that asks the magnetometer just when pressure is missing kept both (entry 74).
- **A fallback cannot be scored where the failure never happens.** The tuning sessions never lost pressure, so the dropout had to be simulated (masked runs), and the a56 held-out test was spoiled by its own sensor fault (entry 74).
- **An LLM in the hybrid cannot fix a wrong local answer.** hybrid_gemini and hybrid (Claude) have the same 13 misses because the keyword or fuzzy stage answers them first (entry 75); improving the LLM does not raise the hybrid, fixing the local rules does.
- **Free-tier pacing and the timeout matter.** Gemini Flash-Lite took up to 8.4 s on one of 129 calls, so an 8 s timeout stopped a run once; use `--sleep-s 4.5` and `GEMINI_TIMEOUT_S=30` for evaluations (entry 75).

- **A gate that only removes switches cannot gain where the base rule is already right, and a missed gate is expensive.** The connector gate stopped nothing R0 got wrong on held-out data and, driven by the place guess, lost 9 stops on a clean session because one lift landing was not recognised (entry 76). Check the recall of the thing that opens a gate before gating on it.
- **A chat export is only as complete as the device that made it.** WhatsApp exports carry only media that device has downloaded; three re-exports from the laptop added nothing, the collectors' own exports or pushes did (entry 79). Check the omitted lines in the export text before trusting it.
- **Inventory manual downloads against the sender's list.** Comparing the loose files with what each person sent caught Chris's 8 skipped extra takes and 2 double downloads before ingest (entry 79); hashing on ingest then removes byte-duplicates.
- **A label from timing was wrong twice.** The 3 S24 guesses of entry 42 and the first reading of the two 20:2x S24 takes (entry 79) both pointed at Tian's places; the senders' own names said otherwise.
- **Gitignored derived data does not travel with a merge.** The app crashed on main (`KeyError: 'mag'`) because the night run's sensor CSVs exist only where they were built (entry 81); after landing data, run the build script in the target tree, and make start-up errors name the missing build step.
- **Commit WIP steps in long jobs.** Usage limits (HTTP 429) and network blocks (HTTP 403) stopped four runs on 25 and 26 Nov; WIP commits on the branch made every resume cheap and checkable (entry 80).
- **Check a chat claim against the raw files before a re-collection.** "Chris forgot the barometer" fits the A56 never logging pressure in any session; the night floor failure came from Dicta's iPhone (27 of 59 takes without pressure) (entry 87).
- **Scope that was promised and then dropped belongs in the log.** The user-test kit exists on its own branch and was dropped by the user; the report must say so (entry 83).
- **A hard-iron offset can be found without labels, but check it over time.** A circle fitted to the take-mean x/y of one phone's flat stop takes found the A56 night offset (-32.5, -84.6 uT) that matched the offset measured against the first run, but the offset changed after a strong field at 22:59, so the corrected takes stayed below the phone's normal takes (16/32 at best vs 30/53). Correcting phones without a fault cost ip18 5 to 13 takes (entry 77).
- **A rerun is the noise floor.** Rerunning the committed fused kNN on identical input tables moved the total by 1 take and changed the weights in 5 of 23 folds; option differences of a few takes on the unaffected phones are within that (entry 77).

- **Split a shared node into separate edges, not separate nodes.** The lift and the stairs share one landing in our data; two parallel edges with their own costs let a mode drop one without moving any place, and the research graph stays untouched (entry 78).
- **Re-check an offline winner with the features the product really computes, and apply a decision rule per phone group.** Rule (d) was chosen on day-3 5 s windows; with the app's 2 s windows it kept the iPhone result exactly (25 night 30/32, no loss on complete-pressure units) but one 25-spots stop it had fixed came back. The no-barometer half of the same rule never passed, so the app gates (d) by phone model instead of turning it on everywhere (entry 90).
- **Test the pooled case, not only the cross case.** "Is the other phone worse alone?" (ip16 only: 57/85 vs 65/85 on ip18) does not answer "does it help as extra data?" (pooled 67/85 in the app model, 58/85 vs 59/85 in the fused kNN: a tie); the top-3 moved in opposite directions in the two models (entry 89).

- **When a user says a label "can't be clicked", check every look-alike.** The working "Nearest toilet" chip and an unclickable badge with the same words and the same pill style sat on the same screen (entry 93); a headless click proved the button worked, and the user then chose to remove both.
- **A from-phrase is only a start if it names a place.** "I'm on 4F", "from here" and "from the lower floor" look like starts; requiring a whole-word place match (no fuzzy) kept all 128 to-only test queries' starts unchanged (entry 93).
- **A shared random generator makes a result depend on what ran before it in the same process.** The day-3 distance scale drew its random pairs from one module-level generator, so running `evaluate.py` before `fused_knn.py` in one process changed 5 of 23 folds and the headline by 1 take (178 vs 179). Seed every random draw at the point of use, break ties explicitly, and prove determinism with reruns in a different process state, thread count and hash seed (entry 91).
- **Keep a dropped recording visible.** The a56 night takes were left out with an exclusion list that names every file and its reason, not by deleting or moving them, so the decision can be undone by deleting two config keys (entry 91).
- **A loader that skips missing data silently gives a plausible wrong file.** In a fresh worktree the day-3 loader took session 25spots as empty (its gitignored sensor folders were absent), `baseline.py` still said all rows match and wrote different thresholds; only the `git diff` of `out/baseline.json` showed it (entry 94). After any rerun in a new tree, diff the committed outputs and expect them to be identical before trusting them.

## Open questions / next steps

- **Floor rule (d) in the app (entry 90):** on for iPhones; for a55 / a56 / S24 it stays off (`WALKSENSE_FLOOR_VOTE_NO_BARO=1` turns it on) until a held-out session with a healthy Android magnetometer shows a gain over "start floor kept". The live version asks the vote after 10 s of a stop: scored only on a replay, not on a real walk. `floor/baseline.py` and `results-pack/build.py` read `eval.json` `floor_tracking` (now the (d) default; R0 is in `floor_tracking_by_rule`) and the renamed a56 keys: update them when the results pack is rebuilt.
- **Phone opening the app page when the server runs in WSL (entry 90 README):** Windows port forwarding or mirrored networking are documented but untried.

- **Compare panel (entry 46):** the team should say which training mode and hold-out the report should quote for phone comparisons (the panel defaults to train on all phones, leave one session out); the numbers are a live demo, not evaluation results.
- **Day 3 collection: done (25 Nov, entry 28).** `phase2/clean-data/2026-11-25/` is a third independent pass (3 phones, 26 stops + 28 walks, Wi-Fi 26/26). Still to run: the actual "train on earlier days, test on 25-Nov" cross-session number. Note only the iphone logged pressure this session (no s24).
- **Day-3 data (24 Nov) is in and evaluated (entries 30 to 34).** Still worth collecting if time allows (deadline 30 Nov): (1) a test walk that leaves the recorded route, with "I'm here" taps, to measure the tracker without the optimistic parts; (2) one handset for every session (the 24-Nov "iphone" was two devices, now labelled ip16 and ip18; ip18 has stops in 24 s1 only, so it has no same-handset cross-session test); (3) a Wi-Fi logger that saves every scan. A further fingerprint session with the same handset would add a fourth leave-one-session-out fold (one line in `phase2/new/day3/config.json`).
- **Cross-day with the new protocol (entry 30):** same handset 23 Nov -> 24 Nov s2 13/26 and the reverse 18/42 (chance 3.8%, 26 places), up from 18 to 24% on 17 Nov, still below the 23-Nov 78% revisit number. Only one same-handset day pair exists, so this is one estimate.
- **Open question: does a scan-every-time Wi-Fi logger make Wi-Fi usable?** Still untested; the 24-Nov WiGLE logs are again new-or-stronger only (entry 31).
- **Tilt at stops (entry 30):** answered for this protocol: no clear gain over raw x/y/z when the phone is held the same way. It might still matter for a user who holds the phone differently; untested.
- **17-Nov labels:** 8 of 23 place matches rest on route order. Anyone who remembers that walk can help (see section f of the team summary).
- **Floor-4 Google Maps pins** are still missing, so the map and routing stop at floor 5.
- **Unmerged branches** `prep/training`, `prep/map`, `prep/ready` are still local and unmerged; decide later whether any of it is wanted.
- **Tracker (entry 20):** collect at least two visits per place on day 3, plus a test walk that leaves the recorded route, to measure the tracker without the optimistic parts. The collector should confirm the area groups, the floor-4 loop order and the escalator pairs in `phase2/new/graph/README.md`. "Confirmed" needs a better rule before the UI relies on it.
- **Circle UI and tracker (entries 18 to 21), confirmations needed from the team:** the area groups (`phase2/new/graph/README.md`), the floor-4 loop order, the escalator pairs, whether ACC01 really stood still, the stairs U-turn and the corners, and the unrecorded exit5 to exit10 walk (see `phase2/new/tracking/README.md`). The UI shows these as the tracker has them. After day 3, re-export the replay and reload it in `phase2/new/circle-ui/` (it needs no change if the format is the same).
- **graph-v2 (entries 22, 29):** done: the merge changes cross-day exact by one visit and area not at all (entry 29). Still open: decide whether v2 replaces v1 for the map/UI; the collector should confirm the two merges match the hand-drawn maps.
- **Collector to confirm (entry 30, `phase2/new/day3/names_24nov.csv`):** RESOLVED 26 Nov (entry 69, confirmed by the user): 24-Nov `male_disabled_toilet` = 23-Nov `disabled_toilet`; `y5302_point2` = `y5302_back`; `gate` = `entrance_exit` (now `high`, basis `user confirmed 2026-11-26`).
